Placed on **two channels that disagree about the country and agree about the region**: the owner's `location` field reads `Canada`, and the grant's copyright line reads *© 2016 **NAU-ACM***, a university ACM student chapter. 🔵 **What it is worth in an engagement:** a ten-year-maintained algorithms-and-data-structures curriculum under a licence a studio can fork, rebrand and extend — the technical spine for a CS academy or a graduate-conversion programme, which this base's learn-to-code layer previously had **no North-America-placeable member of at all**. `f/★ = 0.274` says it is **read** rather than deployed: treat it as content to adapt, not a platform to run.

### EMEA

#### 🆕 Added at p721 — the AI Act dates CONFLICT across sources, and the conflict is itself the billable problem

🔴 **Supply nil, second instrument, same answer:** the EMEA leg returned no filable repository this pass. What it returned is a **dated obligation set with an unresolved timetable**, which is worth more to a client than another repository.

| Obligation | Date | Status of the claim |
|---|---|---|
| Ban on **emotion recognition in education** | **in force since 2 Feb 2025** | 🟢 Consistent across sources. This is already enforceable — any proctoring or engagement-detection feature is out |
| AI Office + national authorities **begin enforcement** | **2 Aug 2026** | 🟢 Per the European Commission — **now past** |
| **Chatbot transparency** duties | **from Aug 2026** | 🟢 Now past — a student-facing tutor must disclose it is AI |
| **High-risk** rules for education (admissions, assessment, exam scoring) | 🔴 **DISPUTED: Aug 2026 per one guide vs. 2 Dec 2027 per the 2026 Digital Omnibus amendment (Regulation (EU) 2026/1744)** | 🔴 **Unresolved, and this base does NOT pick a side.** Verify against the official text on EUR-Lex before any client commitment |

🔴 **The classification that drives everything: admissions decisions, student evaluation and exam scoring are HIGH-RISK under the Act.** Schools and universities are **deployers**; edtech vendors and exam boards are **providers**. The Act's extraterritorial reach pulls in non-EU institutions operating in the EU — one report works this through for New York higher-education institutions specifically.

🔵 **Opportunity:** the deliverable is a **conformity package**, and the date dispute is why it sells — a client facing either Aug-2026 or Dec-2027 for the same obligation needs the artefacts built either way: risk classification per use case, technical documentation, human-oversight design, and a logged assessment trail. 🟡 **Measured adoption data for EMEA remains thin** — the one figure found is UNESCO's *`70%` of institutions in Europe and North America have or are developing AI guidance*, which spans two regions and so is recorded as a two-region datum rather than an EMEA one.


#### 🆕 Added at p720 — the high-risk deadline has MOVED, and the two dates in circulation contradict each other

🔴 **This is the single most consequential fact on this shelf for EMEA planning, and the sources disagree.** The EU AI Act classes education AI as **high-risk** (Annex III). Two incompatible timetables were returned by this pass's leg:

| Source read | High-risk obligations apply from |
|---|---|
| Earlier guidance | **August 2026** (i.e. already in force) |
| **Digital Omnibus amendment**, reported published July 2026 | **`2027-12-02`** stand-alone · **August 2028** embedded |

🟡 **The later reading is more likely to be current law, and a political agreement dated `2026-05-07` postponing stand-alone high-risk obligations to `2027-12-02` was reported separately — with formal adoption still pending at the time of that report.** 🔴 **Do not quote a single date to a client. Quote the bracket and the uncertainty, and verify against the Official Journal before contracting.** A delivery plan built on "already in force" and one built on "December 2027" are different engagements.

**Market and adoption:**

- European AI-in-education market **`USD 2.64B`** in 2026 (azumo, vendor aggregation — treat as indicative).
- **Finland, Estonia and the Netherlands** lead K-12 integration.
- 🔴 **Only ~`10%` of institutions have formal AI-use guidelines** — against roughly `70%` of institutions in Europe and North America having or developing guidance per UNESCO as relayed. The two figures are not reconcilable as stated; the gap is probably "formal guidelines" versus "guidance in development".
- **UK:** light-touch, principle-based via existing regulators, still consultative; **£4M** government investment in lesson-planning and marking tools.
- **Gulf:** Dubai's **KHDA** has issued explicit responsible-AI guidance to private schools.

🟢 **New supply signal this pass, and it is the useful part:** two EMEA rows were filed from **state-adjacent artefacts** — [`Altinn/kihub`](https://github.com/Altinn/kihub) (Norway's **Digdir**, MIT, governance *and* enablement in one repo) and [`seanmcww/uk-english-lesson-planning`](https://github.com/seanmcww/uk-english-lesson-planning) (Apache-2.0, grounded in the **DfE AI-in-Education Content Store**). 🔵 **Both are the demonstrable shape an Annex III deployment is asked for: a named, auditable national content source and an explicit governance surface. That is a far stronger EMEA reference than any tutor on this shelf.**

#### 🆕 Added at p719 — the Annex III date MOVED, and a base that cites the old one will mis-advise every EU engagement

🔴 **This is the single most consequential correction p719 found anywhere, and it is a DATE.** Sources on the EU AI Act's high-risk education obligations **disagree, and the disagreement is not noise — it is a recent amendment.** One source still states high-risk requirements applying from **2 August 2026**. More recent sources report a 2026 **"Digital Omnibus"** that **postponed stand-alone Annex III obligations — education among them — to 2 December 2027.** The later sources are the more current, and a May 2026 political agreement is independently described as moving that same deadline.

🔴 **p719 does NOT resolve this from secondary sources and says so.** Both dates are recorded, the direction of travel (postponement) is recorded, and **the next pass must verify against the Official Journal before any engagement plans to either date.** Advising a client to a 2 August 2026 deadline that has moved sixteen months costs them a rushed programme; advising to December 2027 if the postponement did not survive costs them non-compliance. **Neither error is recoverable by a later correction in a knowledge base.**

**What is NOT in doubt:**
- Education AI used for **admissions, assessment and scoring is high-risk** under Annex III. That classification is stable across every source.
- **Article 4 AI-literacy obligations are already in effect** — not pending, not postponed.
- A ban on AI for non-consensual intimate imagery and CSAM applies from **2 December 2026**, which is weeks away and is *not* the postponed tranche.

**National and Gulf layer.** The **UK** issued Department for Education **generative-AI product safety standards for education in January 2026**, over an otherwise light-touch, principle-based regime run through existing regulators. **Dubai's KHDA** has issued responsible-AI guidance to private schools. **Finland, Estonia and the Netherlands** are named as leading K-12 integration.

**Market and readiness.** The European AI-in-education market is put at **`$2.64B` in 2026 rising to `$8.0B` by 2030** — 🔵 single source, methodology unconfirmed, treat as an order of magnitude. Against that, **only about `10%` of `450+` surveyed institutions have formal AI-use guidelines.** The **OECD 2026 Digital Education Outlook** recommends **purpose-built educational AI over general-purpose tools**, which is a procurement-relevant recommendation from a body that ministries cite.

🟢 **Opportunity, placed and specific.** EMEA is the one region where **the compliance artefact IS the deliverable** and can be priced as such. An Annex III assessment duty over an admissions or assessment system needs: a documented human-oversight gate, a data-provenance trail, and a stated purpose limitation. 🟢 **Three rows filed this pass supply exactly those three** — [`Moses-Githinji/meta-cv-hktn-edu-agent`](https://github.com/Moses-Githinji/meta-cv-hktn-edu-agent) for the two-stage oversight gate, [`aloktripathi1/GyanKosh`](https://github.com/aloktripathi1/GyanKosh) for character-offset provenance that is **independently re-validated on every run**, and the [`Asperant/ExamEcho`](https://github.com/Asperant/ExamEcho) reliability engine (Turkish-primary, `719-R`) for observable generation quality. 🔴 **And the Article 4 literacy obligation is live now**, which makes teacher- and staff-facing AI-literacy programmes the only EMEA education work with a deadline that has already passed rather than one that may have moved.

#### 🆕 Re-measured at p718 (2026-11-09) — the AI Act dates MOVED, and a KB that quotes the old ones will mis-advise a client

🔴 **Sources actively conflict on when education AI becomes a high-risk obligation, and the conflict is not noise — it is a 2026 amendment that most published guides have not absorbed.** What the legs returned:

| Claim | Date given | Status |
|---|---|---|
| High-risk requirements apply | **August 2026** | 🔴 **SUPERSEDED.** This is the pre-amendment date, still repeated by vendor compliance blogs |
| Annex III stand-alone high-risk obligations | **2 December 2027** | 🟢 Post-Omnibus. Reported by the most recently updated source (1 September 2026) and corroborated by the APAC spillover leg |
| AI embedded in regulated products | **2 August 2028** | 🟢 Same source |
| Ban on AI for non-consensual intimate imagery and CSAM | **2 December 2026** | 🟢 A **separate and earlier** obligation — and the one that actually binds first |

The vehicle is reported as the **Digital Omnibus, published in the Official Journal on 24 July 2026 as Regulation (EU) 2026/1744**. ⚠️ **Verify against EUR-Lex before putting any of these dates in a client document** — every source here is a law-firm or vendor summary, and they disagree with each other.

**Why it bites in education specifically:** the Act lists AI used in **education access and assessment** — admission decisions, student evaluation, exam scoring — as high-risk. 🟢 **The practical consequence is a build constraint, not a filing exercise:** an automated-assessment feature needs logging, human oversight and documented accuracy *designed in*, and the December 2027 date is the window in which to do it, not a deferral.

**Beyond the EU, the picture is uneven and should be described as uneven:** very few countries in the region had **binding** national AI-in-education regulation as of 2026; most operate on guidance, pilot frameworks, or silence.

| Jurisdiction | Instrument |
|---|---|
| **UK** | Department for Education **generative AI product safety standards for education (January 2026)**; **Ofqual** resources on AI and coursework integrity (March 2026) |
| **Gulf states** | 🟢 Moved unusually fast on formal AI governance relative to infrastructure — **Dubai's KHDA has issued explicit guidance to private schools**. The anomaly worth noting: governance ahead of deployment, the inverse of every other region in this file |
| **OECD** (policy, not jurisdiction) | 2026 Digital Education Outlook recommends moving **beyond general-purpose AI tools toward purpose-built educational AI** |

**Market slice:** European AI-in-education put at **`USD 2.64B` in 2026**, forecast `USD 8.0B` by 2030 at **`31.9%` CAGR**. ⚠️ Single secondary compilation; treat as directional only, and note it is consistent with roughly a quarter of the global 2026 bracket recorded above.

🔴 **Declared gap: almost no actual EMEA school or university ADOPTION rates surfaced.** The leg returned regulation densely and adoption barely at all — the one figure available (`70%` of institutions in Europe and North America with AI guidance) is pooled with North America and cannot be separated. **EMEA is this KB's best-documented region for rules and among its weakest for behaviour.**


#### 🆕 Added at p717 — THE AI ACT DEADLINE MOVED, and the common sales pitch is now `13` months out of date

🔴 **High-risk education obligations were deferred from `2026-08-02` to `2027-12-02`.** Multiple compliance sources describe the Digital Omnibus amendment (one names it **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744**) moving the Annex III date. 🔴 **Guides still citing August 2026 as the education deadline are stale**, and several are still being published — which is itself the opening: clients are being sold urgency against a date that no longer exists.

🟢 **What did NOT move, and this is the live obligation:**
- **Article 50 transparency / AI-disclosure duties remain on the `2026-08-02` schedule.** This is the only education AI obligation with a 2026 date, and it is the one engagement that can be sold *now*.
- **Emotion recognition in education has been prohibited since `2025-02-02`.** Already in force. Any deployed proctoring or engagement-monitoring feature touching affect inference is a live exposure, not a future one.

**In-scope as high-risk:** admission decisions, student evaluation, exam scoring, proctoring and behaviour monitoring. Educational chatbots and pedagogical content generators are covered by the transparency track. An institution that **buys** an Annex III system becomes a **deployer** with its own separate obligations — so there are two distinct buyers for the same compliance work, the vendor and the school.

🟢 **The 18-month window is the actual product.** `2027-12-02` is far enough out that remediation is a programme rather than a panic, and near enough that budget exists: conformity-assessment readiness, technical documentation, logging and human-oversight design for grading and admissions systems already in production.

🔴 **Two measurement gaps, stated rather than left silent.** (1) The player-level leg returned **no reliable 2026 ranking of European edtech companies by country**, so the UK/France/Germany/Nordics comparison is **not** covered; what it did return is capital, not share — **Multiverse (UK) `$70M`** (May 2026), **Ed.ai (France) `€5M`** (Sep 2026), **Medly AI `$8M`** seed (Aug 2026), **Lucida AI `$5.4M`** seed (Apr 2026), **Elephant Company (Germany) `>€5M`** (May 2026), with **AMBOSS, Preply, Knowunity, Gizmo** named as the larger names and **Educapital, Brighteye, Alven, Norrsken, Owl, CapitalT** as the active funds. One tracker's claim that Europe took **`62.3%` of YTD-2026 edtech capital** is a vendor blog with unsourced figures — **do not put it in a deck.** (2) **The Nordics returned nothing specific at all**; the only Nordic-adjacent name was Seedcamp as an investor. Both legs ran and both came back thin.

#### 🟢 p716 — EMEA: **the Digital Omnibus agreement is DATED, the education high-risk obligation lands `2027-12-02`, and teacher anxiety is now quantified across six countries**

🟢 **The date this base has been chasing.** Council and Parliament reached **political agreement on the Digital Omnibus on `2026-05-07`**, and under it the AI Act's **high-risk rules — which name education alongside employment and biometrics — apply from `2027-12-02`.** The Commission published **draft guidelines on high-risk classification on `2026-05-19`**, with consultation open to **`2026-06-23`**. 🔴 **Provenance flag, and it is the same one `p715` raised and refused to resolve:** the `2026-05-19` / `2026-06-23` pair came through a single secondary blog in this leg. **It is recorded as unconfirmed and must be checked against the Commission's own publication before it is quoted to a client.** The `2027-12-02` application date corroborates `T186`'s finding that the education deadline moved by roughly `16` months.

🟢 **And the deferral is the commercial point, restated now that the date is firm.** A deferred high-risk deadline does **not** defer the GDPR, procurement, accessibility or safeguarding obligations that already bind. It defers only the conformity-assessment paperwork — so the correct advice to an EMEA education client is *build the dossier now against a known date*, not *wait*.

🟢 **Adoption, newly quantified.** **Futuresource**: **`67 %` of teachers now use AI tools**, and **AI-enabled devices are forecast at ≈`15 %` of institutional PC shipments in 2026** — a hardware-refresh signal, which is the one procurement line that moves on a multi-year cycle and is therefore worth catching early. **Epson** surveyed **≈`3,360` people across the UK, France, Italy, Germany, Spain and Poland**: **`80 %` of teachers worry AI is entering classrooms faster than schools can manage**, while **`76 %` of students expect to be able to use AI in their learning.** 🔵 **That `80 / 76` pair is the EMEA analogue of `T194`'s North American inversion** — students expect, staff cannot keep pace — and it names the buyer: the deliverable is *institutional capacity*, not another student-facing tool.

🟢 **National instruments, both inside this region.** The **UK** published **generative-AI safety standards for schools in January 2026**, and **Ofqual** published **principles for AI use in high-stakes marking** in the same month. The **UAE Ministry of Education introduced `25` generative-AI prohibitions in schools in February 2026.** 🔴 **Filing correction: the UAE instrument surfaced in this pass's APAC leg and is recorded HERE, where it belongs.** A misfiled instrument is a silent coverage gap in two regions at once.

**Institutions and references:** the **Eurydice** report *“Digital education at school in Europe 2026”*; **EU education ministers' conclusions on teachers in the AI era (`2026-05-11`)**; the **Digital Education Council Leadership Summit EMEA** in Paris (**`41` senior leaders, `30` institutions, `14` countries**); **Fosway Group**'s 2026 AI Market Assessment for EMEA digital learning; and a peer-reviewed mapping of the binding / soft-law / voluntary landscape, which notes **UNESCO guidance is non-binding but widely referenced by national ministries** — the mechanism by which a non-binding instrument still ends up in a tender.

🔵 **Opportunity, placed:** EMEA is the only region where the compliance artefact itself is a billable deliverable on a published deadline. `P114` already wires the dossier on the regulator's own harness; `716-F`'s standards layer is what makes the evidence exportable.

#### 🟢 p714 — EMEA: **the region is the READINESS leader on its own numbers, and p714 measured why its open-source supply still looks thin**

🟢 **EMEA leads on AI readiness, and the comparison is like-for-like.** Workday research puts **`56%` of EMEA CEOs** saying their organisation is prepared for AI adoption, against **`48%` in the Americas** and **`45%` in APJ**. CompTIA adds that **`94%` of organisations are at least somewhat likely to invest in AI-specific training in 2026** — training spend, which is the education sector's demand signal rather than a proxy for it.

🟢 **`714-A`/`714-B` were produced in this region and they change how its supply should be read.** This base's `EMEA` column moved `27 → 30` this pass, and the obvious explanation for why it stays thin — *European public-sector education software is published under a licence the permissive filter excludes* — was tested and **refuted**. Measured at source over `8` EMEA owners, the region is a **spread, not a regime**: `DFE-Digital` **`79%` permissive**, `onderwijsin` `72%`, `OpenOLAT` `57%`, `openfun` `52%`, `edificeio` `23%`, `ILIAS-eLearning` **`0%`**. 🔴 **But one EMEA estate is invisible to every automated licence tally:** Finland's [`Opetushallitus`](https://github.com/Opetushallitus) publishes **`182` repositories**, GitHub's API reports `99/100` of them ungranted, and the payloads are **EUPL-1.1/1.2** — read from the bytes on `9`. 🔵 **So the practical EMEA finding is procurement-shaped: a national education agency's open estate is real, large, and reciprocally licensed, which makes it a reference architecture to read and a codebase to integrate with, not one to fork into a product.**

🔴 **The EU AI Act is now an operating obligation on institutions, not a future one.** Sources in this leg state that schools are responsible for **auditing** and ensuring safe, fair and transparent AI use, and that the Act's **post-market surveillance** duty — continuing audit, not one-time conformity — applies to high-risk educational AI. 🔵 **Post-market surveillance is the commercially important half and the half most products lack**: it is a monitoring and evidence-retention capability that has to be designed in, and it is recurring-revenue shaped. 🟡 **One aggregator in this leg claims the Act is “enforceable from February 2026”; that date is NOT corroborated by any primary source here and high-risk obligations are commonly cited later in 2026 — recorded as unverified.**

🟢 **The European University Association's January 2026 report urges mindful adoption and explicitly prioritising European LLMs** — a sovereignty requirement that converges with the APAC reading below, and the reason [`ollama/ollama`](https://github.com/ollama/ollama) and the local-first row [`munimtechnologies/feedback-commons`](https://github.com/munimtechnologies/feedback-commons) filed this pass matter more here than their star counts suggest.

🔵 **Named instruments** (per `713-G`): European Commission **DG EAC**'s digital education unit (grants and AI/student-data regulation), **OECD** (bias and fairness), the **EUA**, **Fosway Group**'s 2026 AI Market Assessment for digital learning (London, April 2026), **Microsoft Elevate for Educators**, **LEGO Education**'s K-8 AI classroom platform (shipped April 2026), **Instructure** (EMEA AI webinar series) and **ICS.AI**'s national higher-education AI model for UK students. 🟢 **Two resolved to supply this pass:** the Raspberry Pi Foundation estate (`4` repositories, `714-J`) and the University of Helsinki's [`rage`](https://github.com/rage/course-material-agent-skills) course-material agent skills.

#### 🔴 p713 — EMEA: **THE EU AI ACT'S HIGH-RISK DEADLINE FOR EDUCATION HAS MOVED BY ~16 MONTHS, AND EVERY ENGAGEMENT PLAN DATED TO `2026-08-02` IS NOW WRONG**

🔴 **This is the single most consequential regional finding of the pass, and it is a correction rather than an addition.** Education AI is **high-risk under Annex III** of the EU AI Act — a system that evaluates learners or influences access to qualifications falls in scope. The date those obligations bite has been **postponed**:

| obligation | previously | now |
|---|---|---|
| stand-alone **Annex III** high-risk systems (where education AI sits) | `2026-08-02` | 🔴 **`2027-12-02`** |
| AI **embedded in regulated products** | — | 🔴 **`2028-08-02`** |
| **Article 4 AI-literacy duties** for staff | — | 🟢 **already in force since February 2025** |
| prohibition on AI-generated **NCII / CSAM** | — | 🔴 **`2026-12-02`** |

🔵 **Provenance, stated because it decides how much weight to put on it.** The postponement comes via the **Digital Omnibus** amendment, reported against a **May 2026 political agreement**; two independent sources in this pass carry it, one of them a law firm's global AI regulatory update. 🔴 **One source in the same pass still publishes the old `August 2026` date.** The primary record is the Official Journal, and **`ACTION TN` registers verifying the amendment there before this date is used in a client-facing document.** Until then, treat `2027-12-02` as *well-sourced but not primary-verified*.

🟢 **Why a postponement is an opportunity rather than a reprieve.** Three things are true at once and they point the same way:
- the **compliance deadline moved out** by ~16 months,
- the **AI-literacy duty is already live** (Feb 2025) and the **NCII/CSAM prohibition lands `2026-12-02`**, inside the next 12 months,
- the **OECD 2026 Digital Education Outlook** recommends moving **beyond general-purpose AI tools toward purpose-built educational AI**.

🔴 **So the near-term obligations are the ones nobody is planning for, because attention was fixed on the high-risk date that just moved.** An institution that deferred everything to `2026-08-02` has deferred its Article 4 literacy obligation too — and that one was never postponed.

🟢 **The engagement (`P114`), and the instruments are permissive:** build the high-risk **technical dossier** on the regulator-adjacent open stack rather than buying a compliance product — [`aiverify-foundation/aiverify`](https://github.com/aiverify-foundation/aiverify) (Apache-2.0) for the test harness, [`moonshot-cicd`](https://github.com/aiverify-foundation/moonshot-cicd) (Apache-2.0) to re-run it on every change, [`aboutcode-org/scancode.io`](https://github.com/aboutcode-org/scancode.io) (Apache-2.0, **sponsored by the European Commission and NLnet NGI0**) for provenance, [`openedx/codejail`](https://github.com/openedx/codejail) (Apache-2.0) for the execution-isolation claim. **The extra 16 months is build time for a dossier that is produced by the pipeline rather than written once by a consultancy.**

🔵 **Other EMEA readings this pass:** the **UK** stays light-touch and principle-based through existing regulators, with **£4M** placed into AI tools for lesson planning and marking. **UAE** has moved fast on governance and **Dubai's KHDA** has issued explicit responsible-AI guidance to private schools. **UNESCO**'s generative-AI guidance dates from late 2023, updated through 2024 — it is the oldest instrument in play and is being overtaken. 🔴 One market-research figure puts the European AI-in-education market at **$2.64B in 2026** with **Finland, Estonia and the Netherlands** leading K-12 integration; it is unverified vendor research and is recorded as a claim, not a number to quote.

🔴 **Declared gap, explicitly:** the EMEA leg named **no specific EMEA edtech vendors**, no national ministry rollout beyond the UK and UAE, and **no hard adoption rate for European schools**. That is an informed nil, not coverage — and it contrasts with LATAM, where UNESCO IESALC has published instrumented adoption data this base can actually cite. 🔵 **Supply side: `EMEA 27` rows unmoved this pass**, though the EMEA anchor for the learning-record layer is new — [`LearningLocker/xapi-service`](https://github.com/LearningLocker/xapi-service) and [`xapi-validation`](https://github.com/LearningLocker/xapi-validation), both MIT, holder *Learning Pool* (UK/Ireland), now on `../repos/foundations.md`.

**Demand side — the EU AI Act remains the driver, and 🔴 this pass's sources CONTRADICT EACH OTHER on the date.** One education-sector source states the Act is enforceable from **February 2026**; another places the high-risk obligations — the class covering most education uses — at **August 2026**, and notes they may be delayed. 🔵 This base already holds a more precise reading at `T178` (`p711`): the compliance clock has **split**, with the high-risk deadline moved to `2027-12-02` and the transparency duties running earlier. **Where a secondary source and this base's own dated reading disagree, the dated reading stands and the secondary source is recorded as the disagreement it is.** Readiness: `56%` of EMEA CEOs claim their organisation is prepared for AI adoption, against `48%` in the Americas and `45%` in APJ (Workday). The European University Association's January 2026 report urges prioritising **European LLMs** to counter US-centric bias — a sovereignty requirement, and a procurement constraint a studio must design around rather than argue with.

#### 🟢 p711 — EMEA: **the compliance clock SPLIT — high-risk education slipped to `2027-12-02` while transparency bound on `2026-08-02`. That gap is a ~13-month build window**

🔴 **`0` repositories from the mandated EMEA leg; `EMEA 24` did not move.** The region's finding this pass is entirely regulatory, and it is the most consequential single item of p711.

The EU **Digital Omnibus on AI** moved the deadline for stand-alone **Annex III high-risk** systems — education sits there whenever AI touches **admissions, assessment or proctoring** — from `2026-08-02` to **`2027-12-02`**; AI embedded in regulated products moves to `2028-08-02`.

| duty | applies from | moved? |
|---|---|---|
| **Article 4** AI literacy — every organisation using AI, therefore every school | **`2025-02-02`** | 🔴 no — already binding |
| **Article 50** transparency — chatbot disclosure, synthetic-content labelling | **`2026-08-02`** | 🔴 no — already binding |
| **Annex III high-risk** education systems (admissions, assessment, proctoring) | **`2027-12-02`** | 🟢 yes, from `2026-08-02` |

🟢 **Read commercially, this is a window, not a pause** (`T178`): roughly **13 months** in which a high-risk education system is buildable without the full Annex III conformity burden, **while disclosure, labelling and staff AI literacy are enforceable today**. 🔴 **The deployer carries the duty even when the tool is third-party** — so a school buying a vendor's agent does not buy out of the obligation, and that is the sentence to put in front of a university client.

🟢 **The one EMEA supply event is an upgrade rather than a row:** [`fborrasumh/planificaia-docente`](https://github.com/fborrasumh/planificaia-docente) (Universidad Miguel Hernández de Elche, Spain) was re-found independently, confirmed byte-identical, and its row enriched with the mechanism that makes it matter — **the model proposes, the code verifies** (`711-B`, `T180`). For an Annex III conversation, a deterministic validator plus a named human decision-maker is the architecture that the assessment use case needs.

🟡 Institutional posture: schools are reported **mostly in pilot and pre-compliance mode**. Ireland stood up an **AI Office under its AI Regulation Act 2026**. Policy is coordinated through the Digital Education Action Plan alongside the AI Act, per a **European Parliament briefing (March 2026, `784574`)**. UNESCO's generative-AI guidance (late 2023) and the OECD AI Principles (updated 2024) remain the soft-law baseline.

🔴 **Declared gap: there is NO schools-specific EMEA adoption rate in this pass's evidence.** The Eurostat figure that surfaced — **`20%` of EU enterprises used AI in 2025** — is **enterprises generally, not schools**, and must not be reported as an education number. ⚠ All Omnibus dates come from secondary legal commentary and **conflict with pre-Omnibus sources**; verify against the Official Journal before client use.

#### 🟢 p710 — EMEA: `EMEA 22 → 24`, a named-institution university course under MIT, and the region's first SOUTH AFRICAN candidate — which is ungranted

🟢 **`da-w-ai` is the most commercially useful EMEA asset filed in several passes, for one reason: its stated learning outcomes include where AI assistance is NOT to be trusted** — distinguishing use cases that are *“greatly useful”*, *“helpful”* and *“currently problematic”*, and which outputs need strong human supervision. **That is the module EMEA corporate L&D buyers ask for under the AI Act's literacy obligations, and open curricula almost never contain it.**

🟢 **What is safe to sell against, because it does not depend on the contested date — the CLASSIFICATION, which is stable across every source.** An education AI system is **Annex III high-risk** where it *evaluates learners*, *assigns them to programmes*, *detects prohibited behaviour during assessment*, or *influences access to qualifications*. That brings **conformity assessment, human oversight and post-market monitoring**. 🔵 **Note which products that catches and which it spares:** a proctoring, admissions, placement or marking system is caught; a lesson-authoring or content-generation tool that no learner is scored by is generally not. **That boundary, not the date, is what determines an EMEA product's cost** — and it is the single most useful sentence on this page for scoping an EMEA engagement.

🟡 **Separately reported: a ban on AI systems for non-consensual intimate imagery and CSAM from `2 Dec 2026`.** This is a **school-safeguarding** obligation, not an edtech-product one — relevant to a district's acceptable-use policy and incident response, not to a vendor's conformity file.

🟡 **Market and adoption, both low-confidence single-source:** the European AI-in-education market put at **~`$2.64B` (2026)**; **Finland, Estonia and the Netherlands** named as K-12 integration leaders. 🟢 **Gulf is the one firm non-EU datapoint: Dubai's `KHDA` has issued explicit responsible-AI guidance to private schools** — a named regulator with a named remit, which is a warmer entry than the EU's horizontal frame. The **UK** runs light-touch, principle-based, through existing sector regulators rather than a dedicated AI statute.

#### 🔴 p704 — EMEA: the Annex III date cannot be settled from this environment, and that is now a named blocker rather than an open question

🔴 **This is the eighth consecutive pass in which this page has carried the EU AI Act education commencement date as an unresolved conflict, and `p704` establishes WHY it will not close.** The primary source was attempted directly — `https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2026/1744/oj` — and returned `getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND eur-lex.europa.eu`. **`eur-lex.europa.eu` is outside this session's allowed network domains; the hostname never resolves.** No pass can read the Official Journal from here. **The remedy is a network-policy change (allow `eur-lex.europa.eu`) or the OJ text supplied out of band — not another attempt.**

🟡 **The published position, restated and NOT softened by its eighth repetition** (all of it secondary, every claim attributed):
- Secondary sources agree a **Digital Omnibus on AI** amendment moved **stand-alone Annex III** high-risk obligations, education among them, to **`2027-12-02`**, and Annex I embedded systems to **`2028-08-02`**, naming **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744**.
- **Article 50** transparency duties — chatbot disclosure, the clause an education deployment actually trips — are reported to have commenced **on schedule on `2026-08-02`** and were **not** deferred.
- The **ban on emotion recognition in schools** has applied since **February 2025**; undisputed across sources.
- 🔴 Pages still printing **`2026-08-02`** for education high-risk are stale. `p704` read one. **Eighth reproduction; do not propagate it.**

🟢 **The commercial consequence is the opposite of what a deferral usually implies, and it is worth stating plainly to an EMEA buyer.** If Annex III for education really has moved to **December 2027**, the binding duty in 2026 is **Article 50 transparency**, which is cheap, immediate, and already in force — disclosure that the learner is talking to an AI, and marking of synthetic output. **An engagement that sells "Annex III readiness" for 2026 is selling against a date that may not exist, while the obligation that does exist is unglamorous and unbuilt.** `P96` leads with Article 50 for that reason.

🟡 **Held, not new:** the EUA's January 2026 preference for European LLMs, the European Commission Digital Education unit's programmes, Fosway's 2026 AI market assessment, ICS.AI's UK national higher-education model, UAE **KHDA** guidance — all already on this page.

#### 🟢 p703 — EMEA: **the thinnest region on these shelves gained `7` public-sector data layers and `3` agent rows, and the constraint turns out to be the CONNECTOR, not the data**

🟢 **EMEA was `8 / 144` on [`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md) carried into this pass and is `11 / 158` leaving it, plus `7` of the `21` new foundation rows.** What changed is not the search terms but the channel: owner expansion on two European estates (`malkreide`, Switzerland, `75` repositories uniformly MIT; `onderwijsin`, a named Dutch nonprofit, `18`) plus a `topic:` sweep run under this base's own star floor.

🔵 **The structural finding, and it reframes how to sell here: in EMEA the education data is already published by the authority, usually with no API key. The scarce artefact is the adapter.**

| Authority / dataset | Reached by | Grant |
|---|---|---|
| **BISTA**, Bildungsstatistik Kanton Zürich — pupil numbers, school-district trends, secondary profiles, nationality breakdowns, gymnasium graduation rates | [`malkreide/zh-education-mcp`](https://github.com/malkreide/zh-education-mcp) | MIT |
| **Annuaire de l'éducation**, geocoded establishments, **UAI** profiles, the **IPS** social-position index, **Parcoursup** (France) | [`Hug0x0/mcp-french-school-map`](https://github.com/Hug0x0/mcp-french-school-map) | MIT |
| **HOVI** (higher education) and **KiesMBO** (vocational) national registries (Netherlands) | [`onderwijsin/navigator-data-generator`](https://github.com/onderwijsin/navigator-data-generator) | MIT |
| **ETH Library Zurich** — 30M+ books, maps, images, archives | [`malkreide/eth-library-mcp`](https://github.com/malkreide/eth-library-mcp) | MIT |

**Regulation, which is the region's actual buying trigger.** The **EU AI Act** reaches education directly: sources place key provisions taking effect through 2026, with the high-risk obligations relevant to education slated for **August 2026** — 🔴 **one source instead says enforceable from February 2026, and the timeline has been under revision, so this base does not treat any single date as settled and neither should a deliverable.** Education institutions are reported responsible for auditing and ensuring safe, fair and transparent use. Alongside it: the **European University Association**'s January 2026 report reportedly urges mindful adoption and prioritising **European LLMs**; the European Commission's Digital Education unit is running grants and frameworks; the OECD is pushing bias mitigation. On the vendor side, Microsoft *Elevate for Educators* and a LEGO Education K-8 AI classroom platform (shipping reported April 2026) are both moving.

🟡 **Measured gap, stated rather than left silent: no EMEA-specific education adoption *figure* survived this pass.** The leg returned enterprise-readiness data instead (56% of EMEA CEOs reporting AI readiness against 48% in the Americas, from a vendor study), which is not education and is not used here. EMEA education adoption percentages remain unmeasured by this base.

**Opportunities.** (1) 🟢 **Build the connector.** `P93` is the recipe: clone the nearest-shaped server, build on [`cyanheads/mcp-ts-core`](https://github.com/cyanheads/mcp-ts-core), compose the local figure with OECD/UNESCO so it lands against a peer-country benchmark. Every canton, Land, region and province is an unbuilt instance of a solved pattern. (2) 🟢 **Annex III compliance work, and the EUA's European-LLM preference makes it concrete** — a sovereign, on-premises deployment is the compliant default here, not a premium option, which favours every offline-capable artefact on these shelves. (3) 🔵 **Teacher supply, which nobody else is addressing.** [`onderwijsin/onderwijsloket-assistent`](https://github.com/onderwijsin/onderwijsloket-assistent) is the only artefact on any of these shelves aimed at routing people *into* teaching rather than at learners — and teacher shortage is the binding constraint across the region's education systems. A named nonprofit has already built the MCP; the pattern is reusable in every member state. (4) **Curriculum-as-metadata**: [`malkreide/luanti-lernwelt-template`](https://github.com/malkreide/luanti-lernwelt-template) tags learning content against **Lehrplan 21** in code, which is what makes a learning world auditable by a school rather than merely engaging.

#### 🟢 p702 — EMEA: **the region gains a row on a named university**, and the regulatory clock is the only hard date any leg produced this pass

🟢 **EMEA was this base's thinnest placed region (`8` of `144` agent rows) and it gains supply this pass**, on the top evidence class: [`tscheipel/HaDes-V`](https://github.com/tscheipel/HaDes-V) (MIT, `1,105 B` · `3025e7280227`, `197★`) is authored by the **Embedded Architectures & Systems Group at Graz University of Technology**, named in the repository's own README, with the instructor guide deposited in TU Graz's OER repository, the course listed in TUGRAZonline (`LV 525082`), and a poster at **RISC-V Europe Summit 2025**. It is filed on [`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md) rather than the agent shelf because it is a **teaching asset whose permissive grant covers the teaching material itself** — the category `p701` named and this base has few of. ⚠️ Last push `2026-02-02`: a settled course asset, not a live project.

🟢 **The regulatory clock — the one thing in this pass's research with a real deadline attached:**

| Instrument | What it obliges | Timing as read at p702 |
|---|---|---|
| **EU AI Act** | **Post-market surveillance** — ongoing auditing to prevent discriminatory outcomes, with schools and institutions named as the responsible auditing party in education deployments | key provisions taking effect **across 2026** |
| European Commission **DG EAC** Digital Education programme | grant programmes plus frameworks, pushing adoption while safeguarding student data and equity | ongoing |
| **European University Association** position | urges mindful adoption and **prioritising European LLMs to counter US-centric bias** | January 2026 |

🔵 **Adoption readings, with their source class attached, because the strongest-looking number here is the weakest-sourced.** Workday puts **56% of EMEA CEOs** as prepared for AI adoption against `48%` in the Americas and `45%` in APJ — the only figure in this pass that ranks EMEA *ahead* — while the same study reports EMEA HR teams as **the slowest** adopters, so "readiness" and "adoption" are measuring different things and should not be quoted interchangeably. CompTIA reports **94% of organizations at least somewhat likely to invest in AI-specific training in 2026**. 🔴 **The one AI-in-education-specific adoption figure the leg found came from a sponsored article**, and the regulatory detail above rests on secondary commentary rather than the Act's text — **read the primary instrument before putting a date in a client deliverable.**

🔵 **Supply-side and vendor movement:** LEGO Education's K-8 computer-science and AI classroom platform ships from **April 2026**; Microsoft **Elevate for Educators** is expanding across K-12 and higher education; **Fosway Group** published its 2026 AI Market Assessment for digital learning (London, April 2026) aimed at EMEA learning leaders; **ICS.AI** announced a national higher-education AI model giving UK students free access to campus tools. Instructure is running EMEA webinars that frame compliance and responsible use as separate concerns — a framing worth borrowing in a pitch, since procurement treats them separately too.

**Opportunity for Globant.** The EU AI Act's post-market surveillance duty is a **recurring engagement**, not a one-off build: an institution that deploys an education AI system owes continuous auditing for discriminatory outcomes, and almost none has the instrumentation. `P90`'s policy corpus plus this base's assessment-integrity material (`p684`'s EMEA sell, still current) are the two doors in. The EUA's **European-LLM** preference is a concrete technical constraint to design for rather than argue with — it favours the self-hosted, model-swappable architecture `P88` already assumes.


#### 🟡 p701 — EMEA: the high-risk deadline that guidance is still being published against **may have moved by 16 months**, and that discrepancy is the region's most actionable fact

🔴 **Two incompatible dates for the same obligation were in circulation when this pass read them, and both are being published as current.** Education AI is an **Annex III high-risk** category under the EU AI Act. One body of guidance states the high-risk duties — conformity assessment, human oversight, EU database registration — apply from **August 2026**. A later reading reports the **Digital Omnibus** moved stand-alone Annex III systems to **2 December 2027** and AI embedded in regulated products to **2 August 2028**, with formal adoption still being completed at the time of reading.
🟢 **Do not resolve this from secondary sources, and do not let a client resolve it from a vendor blog.** 🔵 **The planning consequence is asymmetric and that is what makes it decidable**: if you build to the earlier date and it moved, you carry cost early; if you build to the later date and it did not move, you are non-compliant in a sector where the customer is often a public body. **Build to the earlier date; verify against the Official Journal before contracting.**

🟢 **Other EMEA readings this pass (secondary, flagged as such):** the European AI-in-education market at ≈ **$2.64B (2026)**; **Finland, Estonia and the Netherlands** named as K-12 integration leaders; a UK commitment of **£4M** for AI lesson-planning and marking tools, under a light-touch principles-based approach via existing regulators rather than a dedicated statute; and Dubai's **KHDA** having issued explicit responsible-AI guidance to private schools — the Gulf's most concrete education-specific instrument. 🔴 **One statistic is worth the engagement's attention precisely because it is a gap: only ~`10%` of institutions are reported to have formal AI-use guidelines.** Adoption is ahead of governance here as it is in LATAM.
🟡 **Africa and most of Eastern Europe returned nothing education-specific on the mandated leg.** Recorded as an **informed gap**: EMEA coverage on this page is EU + UK + Gulf, and should not be read as the region.

🟢 **New at p701 — two placed supply findings, both on named-institution or named-national-content evidence.** [`xieyanran/chalmers-calendar-sync`](https://github.com/xieyanran/chalmers-calendar-sync) (MIT, **`53★`**, the highest-starred novel repository filed this pass) reads **TimeEdit** as well as Canvas — TimeEdit being the timetabling system across much of Nordic and UK higher education and **the only European scheduling read path on these shelves**. [`yusuferdemyamali/engelsiz`](https://github.com/yusuferdemyamali/engelsiz) (MIT, `5★`) ships **Turkish Wikipedia, maps of Türkiye** and a **fully local AI assistant** for offline use — effectively a Kolibri for Türkiye, with the on-device model Kolibri lacks.
🔵 **Opportunity:** EMEA's distinguishing requirement is **documentary** (conformity assessment, oversight records, registration) and increasingly **sovereign** (local model, local data). `engelsiz` is the shape that satisfies both at once, and `chalmers-calendar-sync` is the hook into scheduling data that EU student-facing products need and American-built tools do not have.

🔵 **Everything else it carried is already here:** the **European University Association**'s January 2026 report urging European LLMs, the **OECD Digital Education Outlook 2026**, **Fosway Group**'s April 2026 AI market assessment for digital learning, **ICS.AI**'s UK national higher-education model, and the `~70%` of institutions in Europe and North America with AI guidance in place. **`5/5` held, `0` candidate-novel.**

#### 🔵 p699 — EMEA: no new supply this pass by design — `p698` took the UK estate — and the region's p699 product is the **correction of a causal claim this page was already reasoning from**

🔧 **The correction first, because it changes how this section should be read.** `p698` measured UK DfE at **`515 / 828` MIT** against Finland's **`1 / 182`** and concluded that permissive supply in European public-sector education is *"bimodal by jurisdiction"*. p699 measured a **non-European** estate on the identical instrument — India's Sunbird at **`196 / 212` = `92.5%`** — and **the jurisdictional reading does not survive it.** `T126` replaces it: the operative variable is **whether the estate was built to be adopted by others** (a declared digital public good) and jurisdiction is a proxy for that, not a cause. 🔵 **The practical effect on an EMEA engagement: stop asking which country the estate is in and ask what it was FOR.** The UK's `62%` sits mid-range precisely because its estate is genuinely mixed — shared libraries MIT, individual services often not — so it must still be cleared repository by repository, while a DPG estate can be cleared in bulk (`T128`).

🟢 **Demand — the regulatory clock, which remains the region's strongest buying trigger.** The **EU AI Act** is the central force: education is an **Annex III high-risk** use, obligations phase in during **2026**, and the Act imposes **post-market surveillance** — continuing audit duties rather than a one-off conformity exercise, which is why schools and universities are described as now responsible for auditing their own AI use. 🟢 The **European University Association**'s **January 2026** report urges mindful adoption and explicitly prioritises **European LLMs to counter US-centric bias** — a named institutional demand signal for the sovereign-stack deliverable `P74` and `P67` already describe.

🔴 **A date hazard this base has been caught by before, and it is re-flagged rather than quietly repeated.** One aggregator read this pass asserts AI Act enforcement *"began in February 2026"*; **p699 did not verify that against the European Commission's own text and does not publish it as a fact.** `p692` recorded this exact failure — a superseded Annex III date reproduced from secondary sources for a **sixth** time — so the standing instruction holds: **EU AI Act dates are quoted only from the Commission's consolidated text, never from a news aggregator or a vendor blog.** The high-risk timetable has been amended more than once.

🔵 **Declared gap:** the mandated `AI education EMEA 2026` leg returned **no new supply-side address and no primary regulatory document** — it returned trend pieces, vendor webinars and sponsored content, with the named players being commercial (Instructure, LEGO Education, Microsoft, Fosway). **The EMEA keyword leg is saturated for supply; the authority-enumeration channel is the one that produces here**, and `p698` demonstrated that on the UK estate. Next candidates, unprobed: Netherlands (`Kennisnet`, `SURF`), Norway (`Sikt`), Denmark, Estonia (`HARNO`), Germany (`dBildungscloud`/`hpi-schul-cloud`), Spain (`INTEF`).

Education AI that evaluates learners or gates access to qualifications is Annex III high-risk and needs conformity assessment before deployment. **Held, not new.** Secondary sources put the European AI-in-education market at `$2.64B` (`2026`) and claim only `10 %` of `450+` institutions surveyed have formal AI guidelines; both are vendor-aggregate figures and are recorded as directional.

Neither has been read against the Official Journal text, which this session's network policy does not reach.** The newer reading is the more likely current one and **must not be quoted to a client on this page's authority.** 🔵 **What does not change under either reading:** education systems that evaluate learners or gate access to qualifications are **Annex III high-risk**, with conformity assessment and human-oversight duties — so `P67`'s auditable-tutoring deliverable is unaffected in substance and only its deadline is in question. A postponement is also a **commercial** fact: it removes the forcing function, and a client who was buying compliance in 2026 may now buy nothing until 2027.

#### 🟢 p696 — EMEA: the demand leg nils, and the region gains the two assets that make an Annex III conversation defensible — one of them a national AI institute's, the other a ministry's own production service

🔴 **The leg, measured:** `5` checkable facts (EU AI Act Annex III high-risk for education; the **Digital Omnibus** moving stand-alone Annex III to **2 Dec 2027** and embedded to **2 Aug 2028**; European AI-in-education market **`$2.64 B`** 2026; UK light-touch + **`£4 M`** for lesson-planning and marking tools; Dubai **KHDA** guidance to private schools; OECD **2026 Digital Education Outlook** favouring purpose-built educational AI). **`5 / 5` already held.** Nil.

🟢 **Three rows gained, and the first two change what a studio can promise a European client.** [`UKGovernmentBEIS/inspect_ai`](https://github.com/UKGovernmentBEIS/inspect_ai) — **MIT**, **`3,201★`**, the **UK AI Security Institute's** evaluation framework with `200+` pre-built evals and **model-graded scoring**; [`DFE-Digital/education-benchmarking-and-insights`](https://github.com/DFE-Digital/education-benchmarking-and-insights) — **MIT**, the **UK Department for Education's** live schools financial benchmarking service (FBIT); [`krzysztofbury/librus-mcp`](https://github.com/krzysztofbury/librus-mcp) — **MIT**, an agent surface over **Librus Synergia**, Poland's dominant school gradebook, `39` tools at `v2.5.3`.

🔵 **Why `inspect_ai` is a commercial unlock rather than a tool.** Education grading and admissions are **Annex III high-risk**. Until now this page could name the obligation and not the instrument. A **government AI institute's own MIT-licensed, model-graded evaluation harness** is the artefact a European procurement will accept as evidence of systematic accuracy testing — and the delayed `2027`/`2028` dates mean a studio building now has a genuine window to make conformity a differentiator instead of a scramble.

🔵 **And `FBIT` answers the thinner question of who pays.** This base's EMEA holdings are heavy on pedagogy and thin on the administrative spine where budgets clear. A ministry-operated, MIT-licensed benchmarking service is a credible, citable starting point for the multi-academy-trust and school-group analytics work a studio is more often bought for than tutoring. **Poland via `librus-mcp` is the region's reminder that the national gradebook — not the LMS — is where European school data actually lives.**

The kernel everything links against, [`edificeio/entcore`](https://github.com/edificeio/entcore), is AGPL-3.0 behind a `NOASSERTION` — resolved this pass by reading the payload (`8,537 B`, sha256₁₂ `86b7a74b9496`, a bespoke grant statement).

🔴 **The demand side of this leg nilled.** The EMEA facts returned by search — EU AI Act high-risk dates moved to **December 2027** (stand-alone) and **August 2028** (embedded) by the Digital Omnibus, the **€2.64B** 2026 European AI-in-education estimate, UAE **KHDA** guidance to private schools, the UK's principle-based approach, UNESCO's `70%` figure for European and North American institutions with AI guidance — **are all already held on this page**, checked by name against the tree. One source still prints the superseded **August 2026** date, a **seventh** reproduction of the defect `p692` named. 🔴 **Africa nilled again and the gap is restated rather than glossed:** no African education-AI adoption or regulation fact was returned by the EMEA leg this pass.

|---|---|---|
| The European Commission's **Education and Skills Package** | Presented as the vehicle for *"helping schools make AI and digital technologies work for learning"* | 🔵 **This base holds Europe's *obligations* in detail and held nothing about what *pays* for meeting them.** A funding and procurement channel is a pipeline fact; an obligation is a cost fact. **The AI Act tells a university what it must do; this is where the money to do it is announced.** |
| The **Council of Europe**'s roadmap item for a dedicated **Legal Instrument on AI in Education**, slated `2026` | A second, education-specific European instrument sitting *on top of* the AI Act | 🔴 **Status unconfirmed and explicitly flagged as such.** If it lands it binds `46` member states — a wider footprint than the EU-27 — and it would be the first European instrument addressing education AI *as education* rather than as a high-risk Annex III category. **Worth tracking; not yet worth planning against.** |

(EU) 2026/1744`** moved stand-alone education high-risk duties to **`2 December 2027`** (from `August 2026`), while **Article 50** transparency still bites from **`2 August 2026`**. **The asymmetry is the sellable part** — disclosure and labelling are live *now*, conformity is sixteen months out, and most buyers have conflated the two.

#### 🟢 p693 — EMEA: the Africa/MENA leg produces for the first time in five windows, and two Iberian rows arrive by CORRECTING a LATAM misplacement

🟢 **The Africa/MENA gap — declared nil at p486, p664, p666, p673 and carried as a fourth and then fifth window — returned `5` candidate-novel facts at p693**, all **secondary** and marked so:
- **South Africa's AI framework is non-binding, and the operative instrument behind it is `POPIA`** (the Protection of Personal Information Act) — i.e. an education-AI conversation in South Africa is a **data-protection** conversation, not an AI-statute conversation. `POPIA` had **`0` hits on this base** before this pass.
- **Rwanda's `MINICT`** as the ministry driving a national AI policy spanning health, education and public sector (`0` prior hits), plus a **2026 Rwanda–Anthropic three-year MoU** covering education.
- **Morocco's `UM6P`** (`0` prior hits) and Egypt's AUC as the named university actors; **Alef Education** (UAE) and **MBZUAI with AWS** as the commercial ones.
- The African Union's **`CESA 16-25`** as the continental direction-setter (`1` prior hit — effectively unheld).
- **Masakhane's local-language models** as the open-source substrate for African-language delivery (`11` hits — held, and now connected to an education opportunity rather than standing alone).
- 🔴 **The Gulf fact that changes sizing:** AI is reported as a **mandatory school subject** in the UAE from the `2025–26` academic year, with Saudi AI literacy as a mandatory undergraduate component. *"Mandatory subject"* had **`0` hits** on this base. **A mandated subject is a curriculum-and-teacher-training procurement, which is a different engagement shape from the governance-and-conformity work this page sells into Europe.**

[`groundworklms/coursewright`](https://github.com/groundworklms/coursewright) (Apache-2.0, operative body verbatim) is that artefact — **a generator that token-overlap-checks every output against its own source passage and returns `{"refused": true, "reason": "…"}` when the grounding fails**, before anything ships. Paired with a red-team run from [`game-the-llm-reviewer`](https://github.com/Michael-Jiahao-Zhang/game-the-llm-reviewer), it produces two documents a conformity file can actually use: a refusal log and a measured robustness result. **`P72` is the engagement shape; EMEA is its home market and `2 December 2027` is the forcing function.**

#### 🔵 p690 — EMEA: the leg nilled, and this pass's finding is that **Europe's public-sector education software reads as unlicensed to every scanner**

🔴 **The demand leg returned nothing new.** Held: the Digital Omnibus amendments (adopted June 2026, in force **27 July 2026**) moving stand-alone **Annex III** high-risk obligations to **2 December 2027** and AI embedded in regulated products to **2 August 2028**; the 7 May 2026 Council/Parliament political agreement behind that `16`-month delay; the Commission's 2026-updated ethical guidelines for educators via the European Digital Education Hub; the AI Continent Action Plan naming education for AI uptake; UNESCO's `68` countries regulating AI in education (up from `14` in 2021); the UK's light-touch, existing-regulator posture. 🔴 **Dubai's KHDA guidance to private schools remains secondary-source only** — a standing EMEA debt, unchanged. 🔴 **No African datapoint was returned by this leg at all**, against a shelf that holds four named African programmes from `p677`; that is a leg failure, not an absence of activity.

🟢 **The novel EMEA finding of this pass is a supply-side legal fact with direct procurement consequence.** `p690` audited every shelved subject against GitHub's own `spdx_id` and found that **all `11` [`Opetushallitus`](https://github.com/Opetushallitus) repositories — the Finnish National Agency for Education's production learner stack — return `NOASSERTION`, i.e. *no detectable licence*.** The grants are real and short: [`koski`](https://github.com/Opetushallitus/koski) `LICENSE.txt` **`653 B`**, *Licensed under the EUPL, **Version 1.1** or ...*, © 2015; [`ataru`](https://github.com/Opetushallitus/ataru) `LICENSE.md` **`296 B`**, *EUPL **Version 1.2** or ...*, © 2025. The same holds for [`kouta-backend`](https://github.com/Opetushallitus/kouta-backend) (`295 B`), [`oph-design-system`](https://github.com/Opetushallitus/oph-design-system) (`650 B`), `ehoks`, `eperusteet`, `eperusteet-ylops`, `ludos`, `suorituspalvelu`, `varda` and `organisaatio`.

🔵 **Why this is an engagement and not a curiosity.** The EUPL is the licence European public bodies publish under, and it is the one family on this shelf that **no automated composition-analysis tool will attribute**. A client's licence gate does not report *copyleft, price it* — it reports **`unknown licence`**, which is the output that stops a review dead, because unknown cannot be risk-assessed. 🟢 **Three separate things can therefore be sold into an EMEA public-sector or university engagement, and none of them require writing an agent:** (i) a **licence attestation** over the estate that resolves every `unknown` to a named family with payload evidence; (ii) the **EUPL reciprocity boundary** priced properly — the EUPL's own definition of *Distribution or Communication* reaches making the Work available over a network, so the boundary is not GPL's; and (iii) the **version split**, which this pass measured and which matters: `koski` is **EUPL-1.1**-or-later and `ataru` is **EUPL-1.2**-or-later, and they are not the same instrument. See `690-P` in [`compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md).

🟢 **Two EMEA additions this pass, both from the GitHub index, and the first is the more important thing this base has filed for EMEA in several passes.** [`ideas-edu/ideas`](https://github.com/ideas-edu/ideas) — Apache-2.0, `28★`, a joint project of the **Open University of the Netherlands and Utrecht University**, pushed `2026-09-05` after eleven years — builds **domain reasoners**: components that *compute* the next correct step in mathematics, linear algebra, logic or Haskell, and the feedback for a wrong one, rather than generating it.

🔵 **Why that is an EMEA-shaped asset specifically.** Annex III makes a school the *deployer* of a high-risk system when AI touches assessment or personalised learning, and a deployer has to be able to explain an output. **A generated hint cannot be explained; a computed one can be traced to the rule that produced it.** A reasoner also narrows what the LLM is trusted with — language and dialogue, not correctness — which is the cheapest available route to an auditable tutoring product in this jurisdiction. Paired with [`DavidLMS/learnmcp-xapi`](https://github.com/DavidLMS/learnmcp-xapi) (MIT) writing every interaction to an xAPI store, a studio can show *what was said, why it was said, and that the record exists*. See `../compose/patterns.md` `P67`.

Second addition: [`smlfg/ilias-mcp`](https://github.com/smlfg/ilias-mcp) (MIT, `1★`, new to this entire base) — the first **ILIAS** address anywhere in this KB, naming Hochschule Heilbronn, Universität Mannheim and HS Mannheim. ILIAS is a major German-speaking university LMS and this base had no agent surface for it at all. ⚠️ `1★`, three weeks old, assignments and uploads unimplemented: a starting point, not a dependency.

#### 🆕 p688 — Germany is running a 16-state school AI platform in the open, and this base held neither of its names

🟢 **[`FWU-DE/ais-chat`](https://github.com/FWU-DE/ais-chat) — `AIS.chat`, formerly `telli`** — now filed on [`../verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md). The facts that matter to an EMEA engagement:

| | |
|---|---|
| What it is | The AI chatbot for German schools. Multi-LLM gateway, GDPR-compliant hosting, adaptable per Land |
| Programme | A sub-project of **AIS** (*Adaptives Intelligentes System*) under **DigitalPakt Schule**, developed openly since **November 2024** |
| Who runs it | The **FWU**, the media institute of **all 16 Bundesländer**, acting on their behalf |
| Reach | **12 of 16 states released as of 09/2026** (secondary); Baden-Württemberg, Bayern, Brandenburg, Bremen, Hessen, Niedersachsen, NRW, Schleswig-Holstein and the Saarland each named live in at least one source |
| Licence | 🔴 **AGPL-3.0** (`34,523 B`, sha256₁₂ `8486a10c4393`, read byte-for-byte; `gh api` agrees) |
| Adoption signal | `27★` — **adoption here is counted in ministries, not stars**, and a star count is the wrong instrument for a public-sector platform |

🔴 **The retrieval lesson is as valuable as the platform.** It was **renamed from `telli` to `AIS.chat` in May 2026 for trademark reasons**, and this base held *neither* string: `AIS.chat` greps to `0` across the tree, and every apparent `telli` hit is a substring of *intelligent*/*intelligence*. The literature uses the old name, the repository uses the new one, and a leg that searches either alone sees half the story. **A product rename is a retrieval failure mode this base has no guard against.**

🟢 **The commercial shape, stated against the licence:** AGPL network-use means a hosted, client-branded fork owes modified source to its own users — teachers and pupils. For a ministry that is often fine; for a resold differentiated product it is disqualifying. **The buildable motion is integration across a licence boundary** (curriculum, assessment, LMS and analytics layers as separate services with documented interfaces), not forking.

#### 🆕 p688 — Three further German datapoints, two of them procurement-scale

- 🟢 **KIMADU** (University of Siegen with the NRW school ministry) — the clearest *didactic* agent taxonomy this base has seen from a public programme: agents split into **`Lehrpartner`** (teacher-side, lesson planning) and **`Lernpartner`** (pupil-side), plus a **`KRAFT+`-Agent**, described as a **meta-agent — "an agent for agents"** — for teachers. 🔵 **Worth reusing as vocabulary in an EMEA public-sector proposal**: it maps exactly onto the teacher-side/learner-side split this KB's own shelves already use (`canvas-grader-mcp` versus `canvas-lms-student-mcp`). No public repository was located; recorded as a programme, not an address.
- 🟢 **KoDaS** — **six Bundesländer** signed a framework contract for an open-source education platform (Nextcloud-based) worth **up to €67.5M**. 🔵 Not AI, and that is the point: it sizes what German states will actually sign for open-source education infrastructure, and it is the collaboration/storage substrate an AI layer would sit beside.
- 🟢 **Baden-Württemberg's `F13`** — the state's own AI assistant runs **open-weight models in a BITBW-operated environment**. Another instance of the sovereign-hosting pattern this base tracks, now with a named state operator.

Exam-integrity tooling is where EU high-risk assessment obligations will bite first, and the file-level MPL-2.0 boundary is the one copyleft shape that survives a public-sector procurement review intact.

##### 🆕 p686 — The AI Act high-risk deadline MOVED, and selling urgency off the old date is now selling a date that does not exist

🔴 **This is the most consequential correction of the pass.** Education AI is Annex III high-risk under the EU AI Act, and the widely-quoted compliance date was **2 August 2026**. The **Digital Omnibus amendment, Regulation (EU) 2026/1744**, moved it: **2 December 2027** for stand-alone Annex III systems and **2 August 2028** for AI embedded in regulated products. Vendor material published earlier in 2026 — including sources returned by this pass's own search leg — still quotes August 2026. 🟢 **What did NOT move: the prohibition on AI-generated non-consensual intimate imagery and CSAM takes effect `2 December 2026`**, i.e. **inside the next month**, and it binds any deployment with a generative image surface pointed at minors. For a schools engagement that is the live deadline, not the high-risk one.

🟢 **Commercially this is a reprieve that changes what to sell.** An extra `16` months removes the compliance-panic trigger, so EMEA education AI reverts to being bought on instructional value. The OECD's **2026 Digital Education Outlook** says the same thing from the demand side: move **beyond general-purpose AI** toward purpose-built educational systems. Market figure found: **$2.64 B** for European AI-in-education in 2026 — single vendor source, uncorroborated, flagged.

🟢 **The permissive-licence asymmetry is sharpest in this region, and p686 sharpened it further.** European institutional procurement rewards sovereignty and self-hosting, while the dominant LMS estate is copyleft — Moodle GPL-3.0, Open edX AGPL-3.0. **OpenOlat (Apache-2.0, `10,982 B`, `6dbc5b605ed0`, re-verified byte-exact at p686) is the only major institutional LMS that permits a closed commercial derivative**, and it is **Swiss** — `frentix GmbH` is named in the repository's own README, which is how this base places it EMEA rather than by owner attribute. For any EMEA engagement needing a proprietary layer on an open LMS, OpenOlat is the only lawful base. Leading K-12 integrators cited: **Finland, Estonia, Netherlands**.

🟢 **Gulf:** the regional guide finds Gulf states moving faster on formal AI governance than on infrastructure — the **UAE national AI strategy** and **Dubai's KHDA** issuing explicit responsible-AI guidance to private schools. A KHDA-aligned governance artefact is a procurable deliverable for the Dubai private-school segment, which is large, fee-paying and centrally regulated.

🔴 **AFRICA IS A MEASURED GAP, NOT COVERAGE.** The EMEA leg returned **no substantive Africa-specific adoption or regulation material** — the sources resolve to EU plus Gulf and stop. This base therefore holds **no** African education-AI intelligence, and the `A` in EMEA is currently unearned on this shelf. **An EMEA engagement scoped to Africa cannot be served from this KB today.** Closing it needs country-level legs (Nigeria, Kenya, South Africa, Egypt) rather than another regional one, and the EU-first framing of every source returned is itself the reason the gap persists.


🔴 **`p683` demand leg — NIL. `9/9` held.** Searched `AI education EMEA 2026 adoption regulation players`. Returned: the Digital Omnibus on AI published in the OJ on `2026-07-24` as **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** with Annex III stand-alone high-risk obligations at `2027-12-02`; European AI-in-education at **`$2.64bn` (2026)**; Finland, Estonia and the Netherlands as K-12 integration leaders; `10 %` of `450+` institutions with formal AI guidelines; Dubai's **KHDA** guidance to private schools; the UK's light-touch DfE/Ofqual route. **All held.** 🟡 The sources still disagree with each other on the high-risk date (`August 2026` vs `2027-12-02`); this base holds the Omnibus reading, and the disagreement is itself already recorded.

🟢 **Supply side, new at p683 — and it changes what an EMEA engagement can be sold.** [`MaMpf-HD/mampf`](https://github.com/MaMpf-HD/mampf) (**MIT**, `1,088 B`, sha256₁₂ `c88be2cc0844`, `43★`, default ref **`next`**) is filed at [`../verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) and placed EMEA on `mampf.mathi.uni-heidelberg.de` — a live host under a **national academic domain, committed in the repository's own README**. 🔵 **Why it is an opportunity and not a curiosity:** this page's EMEA platform tier was **GPL-only** (ILIAS, Moodle, Chamilo), so every EMEA university engagement inherited a source-disclosure obligation on the derivative. MaMpf is the **first permissive EMEA university platform** on the shelf — a client-branded derivative discloses nothing. 🔴 Thin maintainer (`85` open issues on `43★`): quote it as fork-and-own.

##### 🆕 p682 — the leg returned **nothing new**, and the Digital Omnibus date held on a second independent reading

The two sets do not disagree about the world; they disagree about **which is the current text**, and a reader who samples one source gets a date that is wrong by sixteen months. 🔴 **Operational consequence for this file: any EMEA compliance claim must cite the Official Journal regulation number, never a vendor explainer.** The primary source could not be read directly this pass — `digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu` **does not resolve in this session's network policy** — so the date stands on `p681`'s reading plus this pass's secondary corroboration, and that limitation is recorded rather than papered over.
