### LATAM

#### 🆕 Added at p721 — the region supplies CONTENT and ADMINISTRATION and imports the AGENT layer, and the VC figure this base was carrying is CORRECTED DOWN by two orders of magnitude

🔴 **Correction first.** This base's rotation-log summaries have carried *"LATAM-$4.2B+$4.1B-VC-265deals"*. Re-measured this pass:

| Claim carried | 🟢 Measured 2026-11-09 | Source |
|---|---|---|
| *"$4.1B VC, 265 deals"* | 🔴 **Unsupported.** Brazil edtech **all-time disclosed funding ≈ `$513M`** (Tracxn) / **≈ `$505M` across `994` tracked companies, `59%` of it in three companies** (Fliarbi) | aggregator trackers |
| 2026 disclosed Brazilian edtech funding | 🔴 **≈ `$15.8M` — essentially ONE round:** BeConfident **Series A, `$15.8M`, 9 Feb 2026, Prosus** participating | Tracxn + Fliarbi agree |
| Other 2026 rounds found | **Premia** `R$5M` (~`$970K`, 1 Sep 2026); **AMentoria** `$196K` seed (Triaxis + Crescera, Sep 2026) | — |
| *"$265M"* | 🟡 **Appears to be a FUND raise mis-transcribed as deal count** — Shizune lists one education fund raising `$265M` in 2026, flagged unverified by the source itself | — |

🔴 **So the real LATAM funding picture is roughly `$0.5B` cumulative and a near-frozen 2026, not `$4.1B`** — a ~`8×` overstatement that would have mis-sized every business case built on it. Brazil has **`1,478` tracked edtech startups** against that thin capital, which is an oversupply of companies relative to funding.

🟢 **Adoption is the bright line and it is genuinely high:** AI use **exceeds `70%` of digital users** in the largest LATAM markets, with **Brazil and Mexico up to `76%`** (EFE, Aug 2026) — and the same reporting notes adoption is outrunning institutional capacity to regulate it.

🔴 **Regulation is fragmented and nothing binding is in force for education:**
- **Brazil** — **PL 2338/2023** passed the Senate Dec 2024, **still pending in the Câmara dos Deputados**; detailed rules including the *"high-risk"* definition are placed at **Q4 2026** by one source. 🔴 **Sources disagree on whether it is law; this base records it as NOT YET IN FORCE and says so.**
- **México** — **no general AI law.** Sectoral and fragmented. 🟢 **Estado de México amended Article 61 of its education law in April 2026** to promote responsible, gradual AI use in upper-secondary and higher education — a state-level, not federal, instrument. The *"Ley Nahui"* is a pending bill with no confirmed date.
- **Region-wide** — no unified framework for AI in higher education; Brazil and Colombia have national AI strategies without education-sector rules.

🔵 **Opportunity, from the measured SHAPE of supply:** `6` single-term Spanish/Portuguese legs returned `30` addresses and **zero AI tutoring agents** — the region supplies **curricula** (`Universidade-Livre/ciencia-da-computacao`, `21,023★`, MIT), **bootcamp syllabi** (`Laboratoria/curriculum`, `486★`), **school administration** (`portabilis/i-educar`, `724★`, GPL-2.0) and now a **national exam corpus** (`yunger7/enem-api`, `366★`, GPL-2.0, filed this pass). 🟢 **The engagement writes itself: import the APAC agent layer, localise it against ENEM items, integrate `i-educar` rather than replacing it, and consume both GPL-2.0 artefacts as network services so nothing copyleft enters the client deliverable.**


#### 🆕 Added at p720 — the governance gap is confirmed and quantified, and the SUPPLY question is now settled: it was the search language

**UNESCO IESALC, re-read this pass — the headline pair:**

| Measure | Value |
|---|--:|
| Institutions using AI in at least one area | **`87%`** |
| Institutions with **any** formal framework | 🔴 **`26%`** |
| Universities with **no specific AI budget** | 🔴 **`55%`** |
| Coverage | `200` institutions, `19` countries |

**National readings:** Brazil — **`53%`** of school administrators use AI, **`22%`** report their school has guidelines (Cetic.br). Uruguay — **`75%`** of public-school teachers use these tools (Ceibal).

🔴 **A `87%`-versus-`26%` gap is not an adoption problem, it is an unmanaged-deployment problem, and the `55%` with no budget explains why it persists.** UNESCO's own asks — fold higher education into national AI strategies, fund it, issue national guidance, put AI literacy and governance into **accreditation and quality assurance** — name the procurement channel. 🔵 **Accreditation is the lever: it is the one requirement a university cannot decline, which makes governance work mandatory rather than discretionary.**

**Players:** UNESCO and **UNESCO IESALC** (the regional policy voice; **Observatory on AI in Education launched April 2026 in Santiago**), **UNU-IAS**, **CENIA** (Chile), **Tecnológico de Monterrey** (UNESCO agreement, March 2026), **Ceibal** (Uruguay) and **Cetic.br** (Brazil) as the national data sources.

🔴 **Gap this pass did NOT close, stated so it is not mistaken for coverage: the status of national AI legislation in the region — Brazil's AI bill above all — and whether any of it carries education provisions.** The leg returned institutional guidance and regional frameworks only. **No binding AI-in-education regulation was found anywhere in LATAM**, which is itself the material difference from EMEA and APAC.

🟢 **And the supply question that three passes treated as a shortage is now closed.** `p719` found the freeze was a search-language artefact; p720 found the mechanism: **`720-O` — `OR` in the GitHub search API is silently discarded, so every multi-term Spanish/Portuguese leg this base ever ran executed ONE term.** Re-run single-term, the leg produced **`5` filable LATAM rows** (`LATAM 27 → 32`), including [`robles311/planificacion-profesores`](https://github.com/robles311/planificacion-profesores) (weekly teacher plans as `.docx`), [`vadolasi/codelabs`](https://github.com/vadolasi/codelabs) (Apache-2.0 teaching IDE, Brazil) and [`eeminionn/tomatin-code-lab`](https://github.com/eeminionn/tomatin-code-lab) (mission-based classroom with the progression logic in PostgreSQL). 🔵 **LATAM supply was never thin. The instrument was.**

#### 🆕 Added at p719 — the UNESCO IESALC numbers are the best LATAM measurement this base has held, and they say the gap is MEASUREMENT, not adoption

🟢 **The region's supply freeze broke this pass (`LATAM 25 → 27`, see `719-N`) and the demand-side picture is now the sharpest of any region, because one authoritative study replaced a scatter of vendor blogs.**

**UNESCO IESALC, with UNU-IAS — `200` higher-education institutions across `19` countries:**
- **`87%` use AI in at least one area**; **`74%` in teaching and learning.** Adoption is not the problem anywhere in this dataset.
- **Only `26%` have any kind of formal framework.**
- **`55%` have no specific AI budget at all.**
- 🔴 **The finding to quote in a pitch, in UNESCO's own framing: there are TEN TIMES more universities USING AI than universities MEASURING what it delivers.**

**School level.**
- **Uruguay — `75%` of public-school teachers** report using these tools (Ceibal, 2026).
- **Brazil — `65%` of internet users aged 9–17** report using AI tools (Cetic.br, 2026).
- 🔴 **Fewer than `10%` of institutions region-wide have formal guidelines AND sufficient capacity**, even though **Chile and Brazil both report over `50%` teacher use.**

**Institutional players, which matter in LATAM more than vendors do.** **UNESCO** launched the **Observatory on Artificial Intelligence in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean on 14 April 2026 in Santiago, Chile**; **Tecnológico de Monterrey** signed an agreement with UNESCO to advance AI in education; **CENIA** partners with UNESCO on ethical AI in education in Chile; **Ceibal** (Uruguay) and **Cetic.br** (Brazil) supply the national data.

🟡 **Regulation — an informed nil, stated rather than left blank.** This leg surfaced **no binding national AI-in-education law** anywhere in the region. The policy direction is UNESCO's recommendation set: bring higher education into national AI strategies, fund it specifically, publish national guidance, and **integrate AI literacy and governance into accreditation and quality assurance.** 🔵 Brazil's general AI bill and Chile's AI framework exist but were not resolved to education-specific duties within this pass's budget, and are named as the next LATAM regulatory question rather than guessed at.

🟢 **Opportunity, placed and specific — and the numbers dictate it unusually clearly.** With `87%` adoption, `26%` frameworks, `55%` no budget, and the accreditation hook in UNESCO's recommendations, **the sellable LATAM engagement is EVALUATION AND ACCREDITATION EVIDENCE, not deployment.** The institutions already use AI; what they cannot do is show an accreditor what it delivered. 🟢 **The rig for that is now on these shelves and was filed this pass:** [`Grvrajput/llm-eval-rubric-kit`](https://github.com/Grvrajput/llm-eval-rubric-kit) for auditable deterministic scoring with mandatory rationales, and [`JacobFV/formal-language-cirriculum`](https://github.com/JacobFV/formal-language-cirriculum) for evaluation items that cannot have been trained on (`719-E`).

🟢 **And the budget constraint dictates the delivery FORM, which is why [`isabela-lima/flora`](https://github.com/isabela-lima/flora) matters out of proportion to its `1★`.** It is a **file tree in Obsidian** that any file-capable agent drives — reads the official `edital`, builds the plan, prepares the day's lesson, records results. **With `55%` of institutions holding no AI budget, a deliverable with no hosting line item to approve is not a compromise; it is the only shape that gets signed.** 🔴 **Counter-note so this is not read as a licence-free win:** the other new LATAM row, [`P0sseid0n/pomoshot`](https://github.com/P0sseid0n/pomoshot), is **AGPL-3.0**, and [`Luisr-nunes/Educativia`](https://github.com/Luisr-nunes/Educativia) — the best-fitting BNCC-aligned subject found — **has no licence file at all at five probed names.** Permissive LATAM supply is real but thin, and it must be checked per subject rather than assumed from the region's openness.

#### 🆕 Re-measured at p718 (2026-11-09) — the best-evidenced DEMAND of any region in this KB, against the thinnest permissive supply

🟢 **This is now the region this base can quote with the most confidence, because the evidence is survey data from named institutions rather than vendor forecasting.**

| Figure | Value | Source |
|---|--:|---|
| Universities using AI in teaching, research or administration | **`87%`** | UNESCO IESALC, 2026 (with UNU-IAS) — **`200` higher-education institutions across `19` countries** |
| Students reporting AI use | **`92%`** | Digital Education Council, 2026 — `29` institutions |
| Universities with **any formal AI framework** | 🔴 **`26%`** | UNESCO IESALC, 2026 |
| Universities with **no specific AI budget** | 🔴 **`55%`** | UNESCO IESALC, 2026 |
| Brazilian school administrators using AI | `53%` | Cetic.br, 2026 |
| …of whose schools have **guidelines** for it | 🔴 **`22%`** | Cetic.br, 2026 |
| Teachers already using AI tools (Chile, Brazil) | **`>50%`** | regional leg |

🔴 **The governance gap is the regional thesis, and it is quantified twice at two different levels of the system** — `87%` adoption against `26%` frameworks in higher education, `53%` against `22%` in Brazilian schools. The same shape, measured independently. **The sellable engagement in LATAM is governance and deployment on top of adoption that has already happened**, not adoption itself.

🔴 **And `55%` with no AI budget is the constraint that decides the architecture.** Widespread use, formal policy rare, dedicated money rarer. That is the profile for which the **MIT/Apache** components on this KB's shelves exist — and it argues specifically for the *local-first, zero-cloud* class of row (`libre-edutech-os`, `MindEtude`, filed at p718) over anything with per-seat inference cost.

**Institutional actors, named:** **UNESCO IESALC** (the higher-education study), the **UNESCO Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean** (launched **14 April 2026** at ECLAC in Santiago), UNESCO capacity-building on its **Readiness Assessment Methodology (RAM)** in Ecuador and the region, **Ceibal** (Uruguay), **Cetic.br** (Brazil), **CENIA** (Chile), the **Digital Education Council**, and **Tecnológico de Monterrey**, which has signed an AI-in-education agreement with UNESCO.

🔴 **Declared gap: NO binding national AI-in-education regulation was found for any country in the region.** UNESCO's recommendations to governments — incorporate higher education into national AI strategies, provide dedicated funding, develop national guidance — read as advocacy precisely because the instruments do not yet exist. **A LATAM engagement is therefore not constrained by a compliance deadline the way an EMEA or Korean one is; it is constrained by budget and by the absence of institutional policy to build against.**

🔵 **Supply-side counterpart, measured at p718 and recorded here because it changes what a LATAM engagement should look like:** a dedicated seven-leg Spanish/Portuguese GitHub sweep plus institution resolution on all four bodies named above returned **`0` filable LATAM repositories** — `user:ceibal`, `user:cenia` and `user:Tecnologico-de-Monterrey` do not exist as GitHub organisations, and the one real find (an English tutor built for the Centro Cultural Colombo Americano de Cali) **carries no licence file at all**. Full method in [`../agents/trending.md`](../agents/trending.md) `718-N`. **Conclusion for a studio: LATAM is a localisation-and-deployment market on `Global` components, not a composition market on local ones.**


#### 🆕 Added at p717 — the region's distinguishing fact is that ADOPTION ALREADY HAPPENED AND GOVERNANCE DID NOT

🟢 **Teacher use here exceeds the OECD average by a wide margin.** TALIS 2024: secondary teachers who had used AI in the previous year — **Brazil `56%`, Chile `55%`, Colombia `53%`, Costa Rica `52%`**, against an **OECD average of `36%`**. This is the one region where the honest pitch is *not* "start using AI".

🔴 **The governance deficit is the opportunity, and it is quantified on two independent instruments.** In Brazil, **`53%` of school administrators use AI but only `22%` report their school has guidelines on its use**. In higher education, UNESCO IESALC finds **`87%` of universities in the region already use AI, `26%` have a formal strategy, and `9%` evaluate its implementation**. 🟢 **That `87%` → `26%` → `9%` cascade is the most sellable number in this whole file:** the work is strategy, guidelines and evaluation capability for institutions that have already deployed — remediation, not adoption.

**Market scale, with the caveat attached.** MarketsandMarkets put the regional AI-in-education market at **`USD 105.6M` (2024)**, with **Brazil `USD 61.1M`** and **Mexico `USD 59.2M`** together **over `57%`** of the region. 🔴 Set that against the global 2026 bracket of **`~USD 9.6B`–`12.5B`** recorded at the top of this file: on these numbers LATAM is **roughly one percent of global spend while leading the world in teacher usage**. Low licence budget and high real usage is precisely the profile that favours the **self-hostable, permissively-licensed** stack this KB curates — and it is the commercial argument for `docling` (MIT) over `MinerU`, and for offline-first delivery.

**Players and programmes.** HolonIQ's 2026 LATAM EdTech 100 has **Brazil at nearly half the cohort**, and names **Teachy** (AI lesson preparation, Brazil, now expanded into Asia) and **Ginia** (Mexico, AI-driven employability linking employers, institutions and students). **Google.org has committed `$4.6M`** across **Argentina, Brazil, Chile, Colombia, Dominican Republic, El Salvador, Mexico, Peru, Uruguay**, targeting **1.25M students by 2028**, with **Sincroniza Educação** (Brazil) and **Educación para Compartir** (Mexico) as delivery partners. In Mexican public education, a **UNESCO / CONALEP / DGETI centres-of-excellence pilot** launched **August 2026**. The **Raspberry Pi Foundation's Experience AI** is reaching classrooms across the region; **UNESCO runs a regional Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean**; the **IDB** has catalogued **193 solutions** in the region.

🔴 **Regulation: the leg ran and returned almost nothing, and that is recorded as a gap rather than as coverage.** **Brazil's PL 2338/2023 is still not law** — Senate-approved `2024-12-10`, now in the Chamber of Deputies, rapporteur **Aguinaldo Ribeiro (PP-PB)**, a plenary vote expected end-May 2026 **did not happen**, and because the Chamber may amend it the text **returns to the Senate** before signature. **Whether education uses fall in its high-risk category is not established by anything this pass found.** **For Mexico the leg returned nothing at all** on AI regulation. The operative regime in Brazil today is therefore **LGPD and the ANPD**, not an AI act. Both gaps need a dedicated legislative search before any compliance claim is made to a LATAM client.

#### 🟢 p716 — LATAM: **the governance gap is now a NUMBER (`30 %` of universities have a published AI policy, against `92 %` student adoption), and that `62`-point spread is the region's sellable problem**

🟢 **The strongest adoption reading this base holds for any region.** The **Digital Education Council** survey run with **Tecnológico de Monterrey** reports **`92 %` of students and `79 %` of faculty actively engaging with AI**, from **`7,319` faculty responses across `29` higher-education institutions**, with **`67 %` of students using it at least weekly**. The survey also finds the region **more positive about AI than others surveyed**.

🔴 **And the counter-reading, from a different and larger instrument.** **UNESCO IESALC** studied **`200` institutions across `19` countries** and found adoption *“neither uniform nor evenly distributed,”* with a clear **private-versus-public** gap, calling for stronger governance and digital-literacy investment. The decisive figure: **only ≈`30 %` of Latin American universities have published policies on how to use AI.**

🟢 **`92 %` student adoption against `30 %` institutional policy coverage is a `62`-point governance gap, measured by two independent bodies, and it is the single most sellable finding in this file.** It is the same inversion `T194` recorded for North America, **but here it is quantified on both axes** — and the deliverable it implies is governance capacity (policy, staff development, assurance) rather than another student-facing tool. 🔵 This corroborates `T187` (governance capacity, not model capability, is the binding constraint) and gives it a number.

🟢 **Regional institutional machinery, and it is real.** **Chile's CENIA** holds a cooperation agreement with **UNESCO** to promote ethical AI in education, including integrating **Latam-GPT**, an open LLM built for Latin America and the Caribbean. **Fundación Ceibal** (Uruguay) and **EdTech Hub** launched a partnership in **September 2026** to help regional systems respond to AI. **ECLAC**'s **eLAC** digital agenda is the cooperation platform, though this leg could not confirm it covers education specifically. **UNESCO IESALC** (Caracas) remains the region's main governance research producer. 🔵 **CENIA is worth tracking closely on this base's own evidence:** `p715` filed `CENIA-DEV/multilingual-translator-app` (Apache-2.0, Spanish ↔ Rapa Nui) — i.e. the same body that holds the UNESCO agreement also publishes **permissively licensed** education code, which is the rare combination of policy standing and buildable artefact.

🔴 **Two gaps, both stated.** (1) **No binding regional AI-in-education regulation** was found — Mexico shows calls for regulatory alignment, institutional reform and curriculum redesign, but calls are not instruments. (2) **The evidence is heavily weighted to higher education**, with **little K-12 policy** surfaced. 🔴 **A third gap belongs here from this pass's supply side:** `715-A` established that the region's largest public-sector publisher, the **Inter-American Development Bank** (`EL-BID`, `79` repositories), grants **`0`** of them permissively. **So LATAM has the strongest adoption, the weakest governance coverage, and a public-sector code estate that cannot legally be built on.** That combination is the regional thesis.

🟢 **Supply note, new at p716 and repeatable.** The Spanish/Portuguese keyword channel **exhausted** this pass (`0` and `1`-stale results — see `716-D`), yet **`4` LATAM subjects were still placed**, all via a different shape: **MCP servers wrapping a named institution's own student systems** — **USP** (`e-Disciplinas`, `JupiterWeb`), **UNL** (`e-FICH`), **IFRN** (`SUAP`/`AVA`), plus a Nicaraguan Apache-2.0 LMS. Searching the *institution's system name* rather than the *language* is now this base's recommended LATAM discovery method, because the proper noun is simultaneously the discovery key and the placement evidence. `LATAM 23 → 25` on the agents shelf, `+1` on foundations, `+1` on verticals.

#### 🔴 p715 — LATAM: **the supply question moved from "is there any?" to "is it USABLE?", and on the region's largest publisher the answer is no**

🔴 **The mandated LATAM leg returned `0` repository addresses for the `35th` consecutive pass.** The one row filed this pass came from an **owner probe**, which is not in the mandate — full method in [`../agents/trending.md`](../agents/trending.md) `715-D`.

🔴 **The headline is a licence finding with direct commercial consequence for LATAM engagements.** [`EL-BID`](https://github.com/EL-BID), the **Inter-American Development Bank** — the largest publisher of public-sector development software in the region — holds **`79` repositories and `0` permissive grants** (`68` `NOASSERTION`, `8` ungranted, `3` `GPL-3.0`). The `NOASSERTION` mass is the bank's own **"IDB Open-Source Software License" (effective December 2025)**, and despite the name it is **not an open-source licence**: §11 permits termination *"in its sole discretion"*, §12 then requires the code destroyed or returned and makes continued use of derivatives conditional on **written IDB authorisation**, §4 is viral, §10 has the user indemnify the bank for exercising the granted rights, and §15 forces **AAA arbitration seated in Washington DC**.

🔴 **Read this as a sourcing constraint, not a curiosity.** IDB-funded education software — `IADB-education-1`, `edu-brazil-poc`, `IDBx-Data-Engine`, `SmartReader`, `escola-no-lugar-certo`, `matricula-digital` (Tacna, Peru) — is **off-limits as a base layer for commercial client work**, because a revocable grant plus a destroy-on-termination clause cannot sit under a client's production system. 🟢 **The opportunity that replaces it:** these are *reference implementations of problems LATAM ministries are already funding* — school siting, digital enrolment, mass document analysis. **Read the IDB estate for the requirement, build the implementation clean.** That is a legitimate, well-specified engagement thesis, and it is strictly better advice than *fork it*.

🔴 **Two institutional nils, with distinct causes, stated so silence is not mistaken for coverage.** **Tec de Monterrey** — named by this base's own intel for four passes — publishes `40` repositories of which `22` are **per-student coursework** on the slug `C.MTY.TC1031.607.2613.A<enrolment-number>`, `21` MIT, `0★`: real, live, permissive, worthless as substrate. **Plan Ceibal**, the region's most-cited education-technology agency (~`700k` students), has one searchable org, `ceibal-tatu`, **last pushed `2015-12-03`**, `35` repositories, almost entirely GPL/LGPL Sugar-era packaging, `1` MIT. The bare `Ceibal` org has **no public repositories to search**. 🔵 **So part of why this column is thin is that LATAM's flagship agency stopped publishing code eleven years ago** — a fact about supply, not about search.

🟢 **The one genuine addition, and the opportunity it marks:** [`CENIA-DEV/multilingual-translator-app`](https://github.com/CENIA-DEV/multilingual-translator-app) (**Apache-2.0**, Chile, pushed `2026-11-04`) is Spanish ↔ **Rapa Nui** translation with a self-hostable PyTriton server and a published NLLB checkpoint. 🔵 **Endangered- and indigenous-language education is an underserved, fundable LATAM niche with almost no permissive supply** — Chile, Peru, Bolivia, Paraguay and Mexico all run intercultural bilingual education mandates, and this is the only Apache-2.0 substrate this base has found addressing one.

🔵 **Demand-side figures re-confirmed, not new:** DEC/Tec de Monterrey 2026 — `92%` of students and `79%` of faculty actively using AI across `29` institutions (>`30,000` responses); UNESCO IESALC — `87%` of institutions across `19` countries using AI in at least one area, adoption driven by faculty and students rather than institutional policy. **ILIA 2025**: `85%` of countries have an AI strategy in place or in development, **but only Brazil and Colombia allocate budget**; Brazil, Chile and Uruguay lead the index; all `20` countries offer postgraduate AI programmes and more than half have AI in school curricula. 🔴 **Still no current binding national AI-in-education law anywhere in the region** — re-confirmed, and now the sharpest contrast in the market map: LATAM has the **highest measured adoption** and the **least binding regulation** of the four regions.


#### 🔴 p714 — LATAM: **the money and the institutions arrived, the repositories did not, and both halves are now measured**

🔴 **`LATAM 22` is UNMOVED on the agent shelf, and `714-I` is the measurement rather than the excuse.** Four Spanish/Portuguese sweeps with the grant in the query — `711-A`'s channel, the one that broke this freeze before — returned **`1` novel education repository in total** (`bgamonal19/one-kids`, `0★`), and **it could not be placed in LATAM even so**: its README is unambiguously Spanish and names no country, which is `711-C`'s vocabulary gap now costing real rows. 🟢 **One LATAM row WAS filed, to foundations:** [`Mangaba-ai/mangaba_ai`](https://github.com/Mangaba-ai/mangaba_ai) (`208★`, MIT), the **highest-starred LATAM-placed substrate on either shelf**, placed on a measured Portuguese-vs-Spanish README margin (`104` vs `39`).

🟢 **The demand side, by contrast, is the strongest it has been in this base's record, and it is institutional rather than vendor-led.** **UNESCO has launched its Regional Observatory on Artificial Intelligence in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean** — a public-private effort, reported launched in 2026 after a planned 2026 launch. A **United Nations University working paper (1 September 2026)** maps AI use across LAC universities along five axes: teaching, research, community engagement, administration and governance. **Tec de Monterrey** reports an *AI in Higher Education LATAM Survey 2026* finding AI now among the most widely used tools by undergraduate and graduate students.

🟢 **Two funding events, same size, both spring 2026, and one of them produced this pass's supply:** **Google.org** committed **`$4.6M`** to an AI-education rollout across Latin America, and the **Raspberry Pi Foundation** secured **`$4.6M`** to scale AI education in the region. 🔵 **`714-J` is the consequence: mining that second name — per `713-G` — produced `4` permissive repositories, and because the Foundation is a UK charity they place to `EMEA`.** **A LATAM funding event is therefore a reliable route to supply, but not to LATAM-placed supply**, and that distinction should be stated whenever this column is read.

🔴 **The constraints are specific and they are not about models.** UNESCO warns of a **severe regional teacher shortage** — which is the demand driver behind every AI-tutor pitch here — while market sources name **poor connectivity, limited devices and low digital literacy** as what actually slows uptake. 🔵 **Those three constraints select the architecture, not the feature set: offline-first and low-bandwidth, which is why [`learningequality/kolibri`](https://github.com/learningequality/kolibri) remains the correct substrate for this region and why a cloud-only tutor is the wrong product regardless of quality.**

🔵 **Regulation stays fragmented**, with countries at different stages of building frameworks (Baker McKenzie), and a 2025 Ibero-American comparative study examines how the region regulates AI in higher education specifically. **No 2026 national AI-in-education regulation surfaced in this leg** — recorded as thin, as it was at p713.

#### 🟢 p713 — LATAM: **THE ONLY REGION WITH INSTRUMENTED ADOPTION DATA, AND IT SAYS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT IS GOVERNANCE CAPACITY AND BUDGET — NOT MODEL QUALITY**

🟢 **LATAM is the best-measured region in this entire pass, and the measurement is primary rather than vendor research.** UNESCO IESALC surveyed **`200` higher-education institutions across `19` countries**, and UNESCO launched a regional **Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean** in **April 2026**.

| reading | figure | source |
|---|--:|---|
| HEIs using AI in **at least one** area | 🟢 **`87%`** | UNESCO IESALC (200 HEIs / 19 countries) |
| HEIs with **any formal framework** | 🔴 **`26%`** | UNESCO IESALC |
| universities with **no specific AI budget** | 🔴 **`55%`** | UNESCO IESALC |
| institutions with formal guidelines **and** sufficient capacity to apply them | 🔴 **under `10%`** | UNESCO |
| Uruguay — public-school teachers using these tools | 🟢 **`75%`** | Ceibal, 2026 |
| Brazil — school administrators using AI | `53%` | Cetic.br, 2026 |
| Brazil — schools with **guidelines** on its use | 🔴 **`22%`** | Cetic.br, 2026 |

🔴 **The gap is the opportunity, and it is a 61-point gap: `87%` are using AI, `26%` have a framework for it.** Brazil shows the same shape independently at school level (`53%` use, `22%` governed). **This is not a technology-adoption problem — adoption already happened, ungoverned.** What is missing is the policy, the documentation and the capacity to operate it, which is a services engagement rather than a software sale.

🟢 **And `55%` with no AI budget is the architectural constraint, which decides WHICH artefacts can be proposed at all.** An engagement priced against per-token cloud inference is unsellable to more than half the region's universities. **That is why `MiniRAG` and `LightReasoner` were filed this pass as the *cost* tier rather than as capability rows** (`T190`, `P116`): small-model retrieval and small-model teaching signal run on hardware an institution already owns, with no recurring inference bill. 🔵 Pair them with [`moodlehq/moodleapp`](https://github.com/moodlehq/moodleapp) (Apache-2.0) for mobile delivery and [`LearningLocker/xapi-service`](https://github.com/LearningLocker/xapi-service) (MIT) for telemetry, and the whole stack is permissive and self-hostable.

🟢 **UNESCO IESALC's own recommendations name the deliverables**, which is unusually convenient: incorporate higher education into national AI strategies; provide dedicated funding; develop national guidance; **integrate AI literacy and governance into accreditation and quality assurance.** 🔵 **That last one is the durable one** — once governance is an accreditation criterion, it stops being discretionary spend.

🔵 **Named institutional actors in the region** (all from the leg, all verifiable counterparties): **UNESCO IESALC**, **Cetic.br** (Brazil), **Ceibal** (Uruguay), **CENIA** (Chile), **Tecnológico de Monterrey** (which has signed an agreement with UNESCO on AI in education), **UNU-IAS**.

🔴 **An external pressure that is easy to miss: the EU AI Act reaches LATAM providers.** A law-firm analysis notes that a Latin American system offered to users in the EU, or whose outputs are used there, falls within scope — which includes a regional edtech vendor with European customers, and any institution running joint programmes with EU universities. The dossier work in `P114` is therefore not an EMEA-only product.

🔴 **Declared gaps, explicitly: the LATAM leg returned NO country-level AI-in-education laws and NO vendor market shares.** Sources were predominantly UNESCO-linked, with some figures arriving through secondary sites. 🔵 **Supply side: `LATAM 22` rows unmoved this pass** — no LATAM-origin repository entered the base, and `763` unheld repositories from this pass's owner expansion were screened without one surfacing. **`ACTION TP`: the next owner-expansion batch targets the `es-419` and `pt-BR` locale signal directly** (`712-A`'s channel) rather than hoping a LATAM artefact appears in an English-language owner's portfolio.

#### 🟢 p712 — LATAM: **`LATAM 21 → 22`, and the row matters less than HOW it was placed: `es-419` refutes `711-C`**

🟢 **The region's product this pass is a method, and it is the pass's headline.** `711-C` concluded that the five-value region vocabulary *"CANNOT EXPRESS Spanish-language, country unknown"* — the shape of most LATAM supply. **It can, and the expression is a standard code the repositories already ship.** [`JhostinAleck/brightspace-mcp`](https://github.com/JhostinAleck/brightspace-mcp) (MIT, `1,070 B`, `15★`) commits `src/shared-kernel/output/i18n/catalogs/es-419.json`. **`es-419` is the UN M.49 code for Latin America** — "Spanish, Latin America, no country" is precisely what it means — and `America/Bogota` is the sample timezone in `17` of the repository's files. **`es-419` → LATAM, with no inference, no name-guessing and no prose.**

🔧 **The guard that makes it reliable: place on the catalogues a project SHIPS, never on the locales it PERMITS.** This repository's config schema also lists `pt-BR` and `fr-CA`, and matching on those would have filed one artifact in three regions at once. It ships catalogue files for `en-US` and `es-419` only.

🔵 **Why this is worth more to this base than the row is.** LATAM supply is overwhelmingly Spanish-language work by individual authors who declare no location — the condition `20 / 27` of this pass's probed owners were in. **Every internationalised project in that population carries its own region key in its i18n directory.** The channel is cheap (`gh api -X GET search/code -f q='es-419 repo:…'`), server-side, and immune to the name-inference error this base has repeatedly warned against.

**Demand side — the strongest regional intel of the four legs, and it is adoption rather than regulation.** The Digital Education Council's 2026 LATAM survey, run with Tecnológico de Monterrey across **`29` universities**, reports **`92%` of students and `79%` of faculty actively engaging with AI** — faculty rising but still behind students. Institutional machinery is being built: **UNESCO launched a regional AI-in-Education Observatory for Latin America and the Caribbean (April 2026)** to help education ministries guide adoption, and **UNESCO Santiago signed a cooperation agreement with Chile's CENIA (February 2026)** on AI literacy and ethical standards.

🔴 **And the gap is stated rather than glossed: no current binding national AI-in-education law was found anywhere in the region this pass.** A UNESCO IESALC/UNU-IAS working paper maps university practice including governance, and a comparative Ibero-American study of higher-education AI rules exists but was reachable only as a bibliographic record. Infrastructure remains the hard constraint — connectivity and digital literacy, not model access.

🔵 **The engagement shape that follows from `92% / 79%` with no statute: governance and enablement, not a build.** The AI is already in the institution; what no university in the survey has is a defensible position on how it is used. That is consistent with this base's standing LATAM reading (`P110`), and `712`'s contribution is that **the region's own supply can now be found and placed**, so a LATAM engagement can start from LATAM-authored components.

#### 🟢 p711 — LATAM: **`LATAM 19 → 21`, and `p710`'s "the supply is UNGRANTED" is REFUTED — the grant belongs in the QUERY. Demand is now the best-measured in any region on this page; governance is the gap**

🟢 **The supply nil is broken, and the method is the finding** (`711-A`, `T177`). `p710` concluded from a clean `5 / 5` measurement that *“no further Spanish/Portuguese sweep will fix it — the blocker is a missing `LICENSE` file.”* p711 ran the **same language sweep with `license:mit` as a search qualifier**, so the index filtered on the grant before returning results, and got **`8 / 8` alive and MIT**, payloads read from the bytes.

Two went to [`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md) with a named country, which is what moves this column:
- [`selajp-jps/TUTOR_CURSO_INTRO_IA_2026`](https://github.com/selajp-jps/TUTOR_CURSO_INTRO_IA_2026) — **Universidad Nacional de Luján, Argentina.** RAG tutor for a postgraduate course whose learners are **in-service teachers**, answers fenced to the course corpus.
- [`TechnoDark-ti/alfalibras`](https://github.com/TechnoDark-ti/alfalibras) — **Brazil.** Real-time **Libras** sign-language literacy aid, teacher-retrainable from the GUI. The region's first accessibility row.

And one to [`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md): [`IngSoft-FIS-Ejercicios/is-ia`](https://github.com/IngSoft-FIS-Ejercicios/is-ia) — **Universidad ORT Uruguay**, where the *assessed* skill is supervising and validating agent output.

🔴 **`LATAM 21` is a FLOOR, not a total** (`711-C`). The region field is a closed five-value set with no way to say *“Hispanophone, country not evidenced”*, so Spanish-language supply that names no country is placed `Global` — **`11` of this pass's `15` rows went there, and none of them is global.** A studio sizing a LATAM engagement off this column is reading the subset that happens to name a country.

**Demand, and it is now the best-measured of any region on this page:**

| source | measure |
|---|---|
| **UNESCO IESALC** (~`200` institutions, `19` countries) | **`87%`** use AI in at least one area; **`74%`** in teaching and learning; 🔴 **only `26%` have a formal AI strategy** |
| **UNESCO IESALC**, comparative | **`45%`** of LAC institutions have or are developing AI guidance, vs **~`70%`** in Europe and North America |
| **Digital Education Council** LATAM survey (with **Tec de Monterrey** / Institute for the Future of Education) | **`92%`** of students and **`79%`** of faculty actively engage with AI |
| **Universidad de los Andes** (2026) | **`716`** AI-related regulatory instruments, rules and proposals across **`23`** countries and territories → **fragmentation** |

🟢 **The `87%`-use / `26%`-strategy spread is the clearest commercial opening on this page, in any region.** Usage is already near-universal and governance is absent, which is a **governance-and-enablement engagement that does not require selling the AI first** — the AI is already in the building, ungoverned. The `45%` vs `70%` guidance gap sizes it against the regions LATAM is usually compared to. 🟢 [`porroto/Agentic-Education`](https://github.com/porroto/Agentic-Education) (MIT, governance-first, synthetic-data-only) and [`ffbris/briseno-ayudame-a-iniciar`](https://github.com/ffbris/briseno-ayudame-a-iniciar) (MIT, no-prerequisite AI-project guide) are permissive starting assets for exactly that engagement.

🟢 **Institutional counterparties, named**: UNESCO launched the **Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean** on **`2026-04-14` in Santiago, Chile**, with **CAF, CENIA, Cetic.br/NIC.br, ECLAC, Fundación Ceibal, Fundación Santillana, Tecnológico de Monterrey, ProFuturo, Universidad del Desarrollo and IRCAI**. That is a ready-made list of partners and reference customers for a regional play.

⚠ **Market sizing is vendor-sourced and should be labelled as such wherever it is reused:** a vendor blog puts LATAM AI-in-education at **`$0.67B` (2026) → `$2.1B` (2030), `33.5%` CAGR**. 🔴 **This page does not endorse that figure** — it conflicts in definition with the global series bracketed above and could not be traced to a primary source. 🔴 **Declared gap: almost all LATAM evidence is HIGHER EDUCATION. K-12 AI policy in Latin America returned essentially nothing**, and that silence is recorded rather than read as coverage.

Three steps, in order: **ask the author for a grant** before anything else; **rebuild from the public design** where asking fails; and **contribute the grant upstream** so the next engagement inherits it. 🔵 **Carry this to Globant's other industry bases:** wherever a region's long tail is individual developers rather than institutions, a thin column measures grants, not supply.

🟢 **The opportunity this places, against the UNESCO numbers above.** `87%` of LAC institutions already use AI and `26%` have a framework — so the regional gap is **governance, not adoption**, and the asset a studio needs is an authority layer, not a model. 🟢 **`udesa-mcp` is that layer, already written, already permissive, already role-aware** (*estudiante* / *docente* / *gestor* enforced **server-side**, OS-secure-store credentials or private OAuth, and provenance + coverage + warnings on every result). **Apache-2.0 means it can be commercialised and it carries the patent grant a procurement gate may demand.** 🔴 **Two hazards to carry into any LATAM engagement, both measured this pass rather than assumed:** `utn-mcp`'s **ISC** is permissive but **is not the string “MIT”**, so a gate whitelisting three licence names will wrongly refuse it; and `UniMCP`'s single `LICENSE` holds **two live grants at once** — titled `MIT License`, with a standalone **ISC** grant below a separator — so every title-matching detector answers `MIT` and misses half of what a deployment actually takes.

That is advisory and control work (policy, provenance, licence and data governance), and **`P102`** is one shippable piece of it. ⚠️ **Not recorded, deliberately:** the `$0.67B`-2026 → `$2.1B`-2030 / `33.5%` LATAM market figure the leg returned again. Its only traceable origin in this pass's sources is a crypto-exchange marketing blog, and this page's market tables name their sources.

#### 🟢 p706 — LATAM: the strongest **institutional** movement of the four regions — and its key instrument could not be verified at first hand

🔴 **The caveat first, because it is the honest frame.** The region's most significant development this pass is the **UNESCO Observatory on Artificial Intelligence in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean** — a regional platform to help states integrate AI into education systems, covered April 2026 as a response to a persistent learning gap. 🔴 **`unesco.org` fails `CONNECT` with `403` under this environment's network policy** (telemetry `connect_rejected`, `2026-11-08T13:44:11.864Z`), so **the Observatory's scope, membership and launch date are recorded here as a SECONDARY, attributed claim and are NOT asserted.** See [`../agents/trending.md`](../agents/trending.md) `706-C`.

🟢 **Funding, dated and specific:** **Google.org committed `$4.6M`** to an AI-education rollout across Latin America, and the **Raspberry Pi Foundation secured `$4.6m`** to scale AI education in the region — both spring 2026. A **UNU working paper** maps AI adoption across universities in LAC (teaching, research, community engagement, administration, governance).

🟢 **The hard denominator, and it is a demand signal not a complaint:** **`56%` of university students and educators say their country's higher-education system is unprepared to manage AI.** 🔵 **Unpreparedness at that level, next to two funded regional programmes, is the clearest readiness-gap-plus-budget pairing in this base's regional intel.**

🔴 **The binding constraint is physical, not regulatory:** connectivity and device limits in **rural and underserved public schools** — which is why two of this pass's filings are architecture rather than novelty: [`Luxshan2000/ielts-prep`](https://github.com/Luxshan2000/ielts-prep) runs **offline-first against a local or cloud model**, and [`skt09/IndiGrader`](https://github.com/skt09/IndiGrader) grades inside a **closed network**. **A tutor that degrades to a local model is deployable where a cloud-only one is not** — that sentence is the LATAM opportunity in one line.

🟡 **Regulation remains the region's dated weak spot, and it is flagged as dated:** the strongest instrument returned is a **2024** Edelman analysis (no country with specific AI legislation at the time; Brazil's bill in progress; Mexico's efforts stalled amid political turnover; *“a patchwork of pilot policies, vulnerable to being tossed out with each new administration”*). 🔴 **No 2026 status for Brazil's AI bill was returned. Named as a gap.**

🟢 **Supply-side placement:** [`kalebhenrique/sarue-unb`](https://github.com/kalebhenrique/sarue-unb) (Apache-2.0, `12★`) — academic aggregator for **Universidade de Brasília** students. Also named: **Ednova** (Chile). 

#### 🟢 p705 — LATAM: **the first properly DENOMINATED evidence this base has held for any region**, and it describes a governance gap, not an adoption gap

🟢 **UNESCO IESALC, launched at Digital Learning Week `2026`, surveyed ~`200` institutions across `19` countries.** This is the pass's best source on any region: a named institution, a stated sample, and percentages with a denominator rather than a vendor's projection.

| Measure | Value |
|---|--:|
| Institutions using AI in **at least one area** | 🟢 **`87%`** |
| Using AI in **teaching and learning** | 🟢 **`74%`** |
| Holding a **formal AI strategy** | 🔴 **`26%`** |
| With **no specific AI budget at all** | 🔴 **`55%`** |
| **Public** universities with dedicated AI funding | 🔴 **`4%`** |

🔵 **Read the gap, because it is the entire commercial thesis for the region: `87%` are using it and `26%` have decided how.** Adoption is explicitly **bottom-up** — driven by individual academics, researchers and students rather than institutional strategy. The schools layer tells the same story: in **Chile and Brazil more than `50%` of teachers** already use these tools while **fewer than `10%` of institutions** in the region have formal guidelines.

🟡 **And read the constraint honestly, because it is the thing that kills LATAM proposals: `55%` have no AI budget and public universities sit at `4%` dedicated funding.** 🔴 **A region can have `87%` adoption and still have no money to buy anything.** The sellable work is therefore **governance and enablement** — strategy, guidelines, teacher capability, procurement framing — not platform licences; and it is disproportionately **privately funded or donor/multilateral funded**, which changes who the buyer is.

🟢 **The policy layer now has a permanent address: UNESCO's regional Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean launched in Santiago, Chile on `14 April 2026`** at the ECLAC-hosted forum, to guide public policy and capacity-building. 🔵 **Named players for this region:** UNESCO **IESALC**, **UNU-IAS**, **ECLAC**, **CENIA** (Chile — working with UNESCO on ethics and education) and **Tecnológico de Monterrey** (UNESCO agreement on AI in education, **`March 2026`**). 🟢 **Tec de Monterrey is the single most actionable name on this page for LATAM** — a major private institution with a signed multilateral agreement is both a reference client and a credential.

🟡 **External regulatory pressure is real and arrives from outside the region.** The EU AI Act reaches LATAM firms selling into Europe, and the commentary this pass returned warns that companies failing to certify compliance **"may be excluded from tenders and global supply chains"** — a law-firm framing, so read as interpretation. 🔵 **For Globant that is a service, not a threat: EU conformity readiness for LATAM-built edtech**, and it pairs with the Annex III classification boundary recorded under `### EMEA` above.

🟢 **Where this pass's filed repositories land in LATAM.** [`CogEvol/CogEvol-4B`](https://github.com/CogEvol/CogEvol-4B) is filed `APAC` by authorship but p705 records it as **the strongest LATAM candidate on the shelf**: a complete course generator in a `2.4 GB` file, running offline at ~`33` tok/s on consumer hardware, with no API keys and no per-seat cost. 🔵 **Against a `55%`-no-budget, bottom-up adoption profile, "runs on the laptop the lecturer already owns and costs nothing per course" is a better fit than any hosted platform on this page** — and because the weights are a separate Apache-2.0 grant on HuggingFace, an institution can hold its own copy.

🔴 **Gap, stated: national AI laws went uncovered again.** Brazil's AI bill, and Chile's and Mexico's frameworks, did not surface on this pass's LATAM leg, nor did private edtech vendors. 🟡 **This base holds Brazil's `PL 3399/2026` and `PL 2397/2026` from `p704`'s legs**, so the gap is narrower than a single pass suggests — but Chile and Mexico remain unheld, and the regional *vendor* map is still absent.


#### 🟢 p704 — LATAM: three education-specific bills are moving in parallel, two of them novel to this base — and Mexico is a measured hole

🟢 **Novel at `p704`: Brazil now has a legislative *pipeline* for AI in education, not a single bill**, and this page can brief it with stages and dates:

| Instrument | Stage read at `p704` | What it would do |
|---|---|---|
| **`PL 2338/2023`** (horizontal AI frame) | Approved by the Senate **`2024-12-10`**; national AI governance system supervised by the **ANPD**; its principles include AI education and public awareness | The frame everything else sits inside |
| **`PL 3399/2026`** (Senate) 🆕 | Reported in **Senate plenary as of `2026-07-01`**, still processing | Adds **AI literacy** and countering synthetic disinformation to the *National Digital Education Policy* |
| **`PL 2397/2026`** (Senate, Sen. Jader Barbalho) 🆕 | **Filed `2026-05-14`**, awaiting committee assignment | **National ethical standards for AI use in public and private schools** |
| **`PL 3379/2023`** (Chamber of Deputies) | `PL 1837/2026` attached to it **`2026-06-09`** | Adds AI teaching to secondary education under the national education guidelines law |

🟡 **None of the four is law, and the two novel ones are at the earliest stages.** Published as a **pipeline to brief**, not a deadline to sell against — a client told "Brazil requires this" would be misled.

🔵 **The commercial read, and it is different from EMEA's.** Brazil is legislating **AI literacy and school-level ethics standards** — curriculum and governance obligations — while the EU legislates **system risk classification**. 🟢 **That means the LATAM deliverable is a teaching-and-governance artefact, not a conformity file**: AI-literacy curriculum, disclosure practice, an ethics policy a school can adopt, and teacher capability. `P95` is closer to the LATAM ask than `P96` is, which inverts the usual assumption that compliance work leads.

🟡 **Held, not new:** the Digital Education Council–Tec de Monterrey survey (**`92%` of students, `79%` of faculty** across `29` universities), UNESCO's **Regional Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean** (launched April 2026), the **UNESCO–CENIA** agreement (Chile, February 2026), UNESCO IESALC/UNU-IAS five-dimension university mapping, and the infrastructure constraint that bounds all of it.

🔴 **Mexico: nilled explicitly, and this is the region's most consequential gap.** The LATAM leg returned **no** Mexican instrument — the only Spanish-language legislative material it surfaced was Brazilian. Mexico is the region's second-largest education market and this page still holds **zero** Mexican AI-in-education instruments. 🔵 **Named so it is not mistaken for coverage**, and with the fix specified: run a Spanish-language leg at Mexico directly (`SEP`, `Cámara de Diputados`), because an English-token `LATAM` query demonstrably resolves to Brazil.


#### 🟢 p703 — LATAM: **`3` platforms filed with a public institution as licensor, and the region's adoption figure is now the highest this base holds for anywhere**

🟢 **`3` of `9` new platform rows are LATAM, and all three are placed on evidence inside the repository rather than on author geography:**

- [`abner-lucas/letramento-digital-em-ia`](https://github.com/abner-lucas/letramento-digital-em-ia) (MIT) — the complete course platform for an **AI digital-literacy course for students and communities of Belém do Pará, Brazil**, run in partnership by **PRODEPA** (the Pará state data-processing company), **Instituto iSACI**, **IFPA**, **SIMBIC/UFPA** and the **Government of the State of Pará**. 🔵 **Its deployment detail is the opposite of what an AI course assumes: a hub of four printable A4 study guides, so a learner with a phone and no data allowance can study on paper.** That is the template for publicly-funded AI literacy at community scale.
- [`Alexsantossp71/redacaonota1000`](https://github.com/Alexsantossp71/redacaonota1000) (MIT) — an **ENEM** essay portal. The ENEM *redação* is marked against five published competencies and sat by millions a year: the most valuable rubric in Brazilian education to automate against, and the most consequential to get wrong.
- [`thyfanixs/conta-comigo`](https://github.com/thyfanixs/conta-comigo) (MIT) — a Portuguese arithmetic game where children answer by **showing fingers to the webcam**, with vision running client-side. Reaches pre-readers that every text-based tutor excludes, and transmits no image of a child — the only posture that survives the LGPD's children's provisions.

Plus [`oscarbol09/thesisforge`](https://github.com/oscarbol09/thesisforge) (Apache-2.0) on [`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md): Spanish-language thesis support under **APA 7** with RAG over real indexed literature and explicit human verification checkpoints — placed on working language and citation standard, which is what the university-sector demand below actually asks for.

**Demand side, and it is the strongest regional reading this base holds.** 🟢 **The Digital Education Council's 2026 LATAM survey, run with Tecnológico de Monterrey across `29` universities, reports `92%` of students and `79%` of faculty actively engaging with AI — which the authors state exceeds global trends**, with faculty use rising but still behind students'. On the policy side: **UNESCO and Chile's CENIA** signed a cooperation agreement (February 2026) on ethical AI in education for Chile and the wider region, and **UNESCO is establishing a Regional AI in Education Observatory for Latin America and the Caribbean**, with a launch planned for 2026. Named actors: Digital Education Council, Tec de Monterrey and its Institute for the Future of Education, UNESCO IESALC, UNU-IAS, CENIA.

🟡 **Measured gap, stated: the leg found NO binding country-level AI-in-education regulation for 2026.** A 2025 comparative study of AI regulation in Ibero-American higher education exists but returned only its bibliographic record, not its findings. Country-by-country follow-up (Brazil, Mexico, Colombia, Chile) is unrun.

**Opportunities.** (1) 🟢 **Partner with the public programme that already exists, rather than proposing one.** The Pará platform shows the shape — a state data company, a federal institute, a federal university and a state government already co-delivering, with the artefact MIT-licensed and public. (2) 🟢 **The `92% / 79%` gap is the engagement.** Students are already using AI and faculty are behind: faculty enablement, assessment redesign and the `P91` marking audit are what close it, and the figure comes from the client's own peer institutions. (3) **ENEM and equivalent national exams** are the highest-leverage assessment targets in the region — approach them with `P91`'s audit attached, because an unvalidated essay score against a five-competency national rubric is a liability. (4) 🔵 **Drop the star floor to zero when sourcing here.** `T130` established that this region's supply sits under it; p703 confirms it again — all three platforms above are at `0–1★` and all three have a public institution as licensor.

#### 🟢 p702 — LATAM: **the best-sourced demand signal of any region this pass**, no supply gained, and one funding figure this page refuses to repeat twice

🟢 **The LATAM leg was the only one of four to return primary and quasi-primary institutional sources**, and that makes this region's demand picture the strongest in the pass — the reverse of APAC above:

| Signal | Date | Source class |
|---|---|---|
| **UNESCO Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean** — public–private, convening governments, academia and industry | launched **April 2026** (one source says planned for 2026) | 🟢 institutional, named |
| **UNU working paper** mapping AI use across LAC universities — teaching, research, community engagement, administration, governance (author: Arianna Valentini) | **September 2026** | 🟢 academic, named |
| **Google.org** commits **$4.6M** to an AI-education rollout across Latin America | **April 2026** | 🔵 reported |
| **Raspberry Pi Foundation** secures **$4.6M** to scale AI education in the region | **March 2026** | 🔴 **see the caution below** |
| **Microsoft** AI for Good research hub in **Uruguay** | ongoing | 🔵 reported |
| **Teachy** (AI for teachers) Series A | 2024 | 🔵 reported |
| Ibero-American comparative study on regulating AI in higher education | 2025 | 🟢 academic |
| Baker McKenzie: AI regulatory frameworks **at different stages** country by country | 2026 reading | 🔵 law-firm commentary |

🔴 **One figure is reported twice and this page will carry it once.** **$4.6M** appears as both a Google.org commitment (April 2026) and a Raspberry Pi Foundation raise (March 2026). Two different organisations announcing the identical amount one month apart in the same region is more likely one story recounted twice — plausibly Google.org **funding** the Raspberry Pi Foundation — than two independent commitments. **Do not sum them, and verify each against its own announcement before either appears in a deliverable.** This is `p695`'s correction discipline applied to funding rather than to market size: one source counted twice is the commonest defect in this category.

🔴 **A second figure is truncated at source and must not be quoted.** The leg reports LATAM as more positive about AI in higher education than any other region at *"92% of respondents"* — and states the excerpt was **cut off**. A percentage without its question is not a finding.

🔵 **Barriers, which are the actual sales obstacle and are consistently reported:** weak connectivity, limited devices and low digital literacy, against a UNESCO warning of a **severe teacher shortage** threatening the 2030 education goals. This base's standing LATAM reading — that **`55%` of universities have no AI budget** — is the commercial frame, and nothing this pass found contradicts it.

🔴 **No LATAM supply was gained this pass, and the shelf stands at `14` of `144` agent rows.** The `topic:educacion` / `topic:educacao` / `topic:universidad` / `topic:profesores` facets were swept at p702 and returned **no novel permissive education agent** — `p700` had already harvested `9` rows from that channel, so the Spanish/Portuguese topic heads are now **spent at this star floor**, which is a measured condition rather than a regional absence. The unspent variant is a `created:` window over the same heads, which is what `T141` showed works.

**Opportunity for Globant.** The teacher-shortage and no-budget pair points at the **teacher-productivity** build rather than the student-tutor build: the UNESCO observatory gives institutional cover, and the barrier list argues for the offline-tolerant, low-device-footprint deployment this base's LATAM patterns already favour. `P90`'s policy corpus is **higher-education and not LAC-specific**, so pair it with the UNU paper for this region rather than relying on it alone.


#### 🟢 p701 — LATAM: adoption is near-universal, governance is not, and `T130`'s channel is confirmed a **second** time — the supply is real and lives under `5★`

🟢 **Adoption, from the strongest sources this page has for the region:**

| Reading | Figure | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Higher-ed students actively using AI | **`92%`** | Digital Education Council LATAM survey 2026 (**>`30,000` responses, `29` institutions**) |
| Higher-ed faculty actively using AI | **`79%`** | same |
| Institutions using AI in ≥1 area of activity | **`87%`** | UNESCO **IESALC**, launched at Digital Learning Week 2026 |
| Regional economies with an AI strategy in place or in development | **`85%`** of `20` | regional index |
| …of which **explicitly allocate budget** in the national instrument | 🔴 **`2` — Brazil and Colombia only** | same |

🔴 **The gap is the whole opportunity, and both independent sources name it the same way.** IESALC's headline is that **governance lags adoption**, and that adoption is driven by faculty, researchers and students rather than by institutional policy. DEC's is that `79%` faculty use is **not translating into pedagogical integration**, and that students are asking for clearer guidance. 🟢 **So the LATAM buyer is not buying capability — they already have it, informally and ungoverned. They are buying governance, measurement and integration**: an AI-use policy that survives a ministry audit, a way to show what students are actually doing, and curriculum integration that outlives the enthusiast who started it. 🔵 **`85%` strategies against `2` funded instruments** is the same pattern at national scale: strategy is abundant, budget is scarce, so a proposal priced against a national AI budget line will usually find none.
🟡 **Regulation: the mandated leg returned no LATAM-specific AI-in-education instrument.** Recorded as an **informed gap** rather than as an absence — the one regulatory item the leg surfaced was South Africa's draft national AI policy, which is outside the region and was abrogated in April 2026.

🟢 **`T130` confirmed independently at p701, and this matters because this base declared LATAM's supply nil six times before p700 found the cause.** The Spanish/Portuguese `topic:` heads, windowed by `created:`, returned **`255` distinct repositories, `224` novel against the `5,553` slugs held, `65` novel *and* permissive** — and **every one of the `65` sits at or below `4★`.** The star floor, not the region, was the nil.
🟢 **Six LATAM rows filed this pass, placed on named national instruments, not on owner accounts:** [`ygorsmc/enem-por-foto`](https://github.com/ygorsmc/enem-por-foto) (MIT, `2★` — marks an **ENEM** essay from a **photograph of the handwritten manuscript**, over **WhatsApp and Telegram**); [`LucasSpinola/enem-llm-benchmark`](https://github.com/LucasSpinola/enem-llm-benchmark) (MIT — free-model accuracy on ENEM **by subject area**); [`matheusmontelro/plataforma-agentes-ia`](https://github.com/matheusmontelro/plataforma-agentes-ia) (MIT — educational agent platform built *para instalação própria*); [`JimmyAlter/estudia`](https://github.com/JimmyAlter/estudia) (MIT — practice against your own campus's classes at **UTN/UBA**, Argentina); [`dduenhas/globo-interativo`](https://github.com/dduenhas/globo-interativo) (MIT, **BNCC**-aligned 3D globe); [`victinjr199xs-cyber/MisterALG`](https://github.com/victinjr199xs-cyber/MisterALG) (MIT — **Portugol**/VisuAlg to production languages); plus [`chiarorosa/projeto-horizonte`](https://github.com/chiarorosa/projeto-horizonte) (Apache-2.0, corporate L&D with RAG and agents) and [`vaniago/kit_leitura_academica`](https://github.com/vaniago/kit_leitura_academica) (MIT, *fichamento* reading skills).
🔵 **`enem-por-foto` is the row to show a client.** Handwritten input over WhatsApp is not a degraded version of a web essay grader — **it is the only honest reading of the actual constraint**, because ENEM's essay is handwritten and WhatsApp is the channel candidates already have. A `2★` repository that gets the channel right is worth more to a LATAM bid than a `40,000★` one that assumes a browser and a keyboard. 🟢 **And `enem-llm-benchmark` is the measurement a LATAM proposal needs**: per-area accuracy of free models on the national examination, which is the first question a Brazilian institution asks.

**`8` live, `0` of them publishing permissive education software, and the single most important nil is `CeibalUY`:** Plan Ceibal is the most-cited education technology programme in the region and it publishes **three unlicensed repositories**. 🔵 **The finding is not "LATAM has no supply" — it is that LATAM's education authorities are not software publishers at all**, which is a different and more useful fact: there is nothing to fork, so there is nothing to wait for. `T126` explains why — none of these estates was built as a public good for third-party adoption.

🟢 **Demand — the highest actively-measured engagement figures on this page, from a named primary survey.** The **Digital Education Council**'s *AI in Higher Education LATAM Survey 2026* (**February 2026**), run with **Tecnológico de Monterrey** across **`29` universities**, reports **`92%` of students and `79%` of faculty actively engaging with AI**. Faculty use is rising but still trails students. 🟢 **Institutional capacity is being built regionally rather than nationally:** UNESCO launched a **Regional Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean** in **April 2026**, and UNESCO Santiago signed a cooperation agreement with **Chile's CENIA** (National Centre for AI) in **February 2026** on ethical AI in education, explicitly to support education ministries.

🔵 **The strategic read, and it has not changed — it has hardened into a measurement.** **LATAM is the region with the strongest demand signal and the weakest public supply.** `92%` student engagement against `0` permissive public-sector education repositories means the region cannot be served by adopting a local platform; it must be served by **assembling permissive components sourced elsewhere** and localising them — which is exactly what `P71` and `P73` already prescribe. 🟢 **p699 adds a new source of those components, and it is not European:** the Sunbird estate is MIT, built explicitly for third-party national adoption, already runs in `30+` languages, and was designed for low-connectivity, high-volume school systems. **For a LATAM ministry engagement that is a far closer architectural fit than the UK or French estates this base holds**, and the localisation work (Spanish/Portuguese content, local taxonomy frameworks) is Globant's natural contribution rather than a blocker. `P82`'s block list transfers to `P71`'s delivery shape.

🔴 **Declared gap:** the regulation half of the mandated LATAM leg **nilled again**. No national AI-in-education statute or 2026 rule change surfaced for Brazil, Mexico, Chile or Colombia; what exists is comparative academic work on Ibero-American higher-education regulation and UNESCO/CENIA soft-law capacity building. **This base should stop expecting a LATAM statute and start treating UNESCO regional instruments as the governing layer** — that is now the fourth consecutive window with the same result.

🟢 **The engagement consequence, stated plainly.** A LATAM education engagement **imports** its stack — the components in `P81` and the sovereign-stack patterns — and **exports** governance: the deliverable the region measurably lacks is the institutional AI framework, not the model. 🔵 **Named counterparts for that work, all surfaced this pass:** UNESCO **IESALC** and the regional **Observatory**, **ECLAC** (ILIA index, eLAC2026), the **IDB** (whose technical note `IDB-TN-3241` argues enabling regulation *drives* adoption), **CENIA** (Chile), and **Tecnológico de Monterrey**, which has signed an AI-in-education agreement with UNESCO.

A Chilean or Brazilian project whose README is Spanish or Portuguese but whose `description` is written in English is **invisible** to every probe above; what the probe *does* surface is the opposite error, an unrelated tool with a Spanish description. **So the `5` LATAM rows on [`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md) are a measured floor on this channel, not an estimate of the region's true supply**, and the honest statement for a LATAM engagement brief is that the regional open-source agent layer is **unmeasured**, not that it is absent. 🔵 The build consequence is drawn out as a recipe at [`../compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md) **`P71`**: for Spanish/Portuguese delivery there is nothing to fork, so the surface gets **assembled** from permissive parts this base already holds.

**Standing recommendation, now on its fourth consecutive nil: run the mandated LATAM leg for the record and plan nothing around it. Budget the pass's LATAM effort on the backlog audit and on the non-leg placement channels (`.br` TLD, statute citation, Portuguese function-word density) instead.**

##### 🆕 p686 — The strongest evidence in this KB for a governance engagement, and it is a 61-point gap measured by UNESCO

🟢 **UNESCO IESALC, study launched September 2026, surveying higher education across Latin America and the Caribbean: `87 %` of institutions already use AI in some capacity, and only `26 %` have a formal AI strategy.** That `61`-point spread is the most actionable single number on this shelf, because it is not a forecast — it is installed usage without governance, already in place. The same study finds adoption driven by **individual academics, researchers and students rather than institution-wide plans**, which means the shadow estate exists and an institution's first genuine need is to *discover and govern* it, not to procure another tutor.

🟢 **The gap is regionally specific, and the comparison is in the same source:** `~70 %` of institutions in Europe and North America have or are developing AI guidance, against **`45 %` in Latin America and the Caribbean**. 🟢 **And the regulatory surface is dense rather than empty:** a 2026 Universidad de los Andes study counts **`716` AI-related regulatory instruments, rules and legislative proposals across `23` countries and territories, more than `80 %` of them initiated since 2023.** Fragmented, fast-moving, and far too much for any one institution to track — which is itself the service.

🟢 **The institutional buyers are named and already convened.** The **UNESCO Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean launched April 2026 in Santiago**, led by UNESCO with **CAF** (Development Bank of Latin America and the Caribbean), **CENIA**, **Cetic.br/NIC.br**, **ECLAC**, **Fundación Ceibal**, **Fundación Santillana**, **Tecnológico de Monterrey**, **ProFuturo**, **Universidad del Desarrollo** and **IRCAI**. UNESCO's recommendations to governments — incorporate higher education into national AI strategies, fund it, issue national guidance, and **integrate AI literacy and governance into accreditation and quality assurance** — define the deliverable precisely. 🟢 **Accreditation is the lever:** once governance is an accreditation criterion, an institutional AI-governance programme stops being discretionary spend.

🔵 **Market size, flagged:** LATAM AI-in-education put at **$0.67 B in 2026** growing to **$2.1 B by 2030** (`33.5 %` CAGR) — commercial research blog, uncorroborated, and roughly a quarter of the European figure on the same basis. **The money is small; the mandate is large.** Price a LATAM engagement off the accreditation obligation and the `26 %` strategy figure, not off market size. 🔴 **A promotional claim that Latin America leads global AI adoption appeared in this leg and is NOT supported** by the UNESCO data; it is vendor content and is recorded here so it is not re-adopted.


🔴 **`p683` demand leg — NIL. `8/8` held.** Searched `AI education LATAM 2026 adoption regulation players`. Returned: the **Digital Education Council** LATAM 2026 survey (`92 %` of students, `79 %` of faculty, `30,000+` responses across `29` institutions, with assessment-side use cases the lowest-adopted); **UNESCO IESALC** finding `87 %` of institutions using AI in at least one area with adoption driven by faculty and students rather than institutional policy; a regional index over `20` economies with more than half embedding AI in school curricula, `85 %` holding or drafting an AI strategy, and **only Brazil and Colombia allocating budget**; Brazil · Chile · Uruguay as the leading group. **All held.** 🔴 **The leg returned no LATAM-specific education AI *regulation* and no education-sector vendor list** — an informed gap, re-confirmed, not silence.

🟢 **Supply side, new at p683 — and placed on a channel this base has not used before.** [`NewSchoolApp/newschool-backend`](https://github.com/NewSchoolApp/newschool-backend) (**Apache-2.0**, `11,357 B`, sha256₁₂ `c71d239df917` — canonical to the byte, `119★`) is filed at [`../verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) and placed **LATAM on a committed ccTLD**: `.env.example` ships `newschoolapp.com.br` and `vps-3769045.newschoolapp.com.br`, and **`.br` is registry-restricted to Brazil** — a registry fact, not a language inference. The Brazilian-Portuguese README agrees but is the weaker reading. 🔵 **The opportunity:** this page's LATAM platform tier was Chamilo (GPL-2.0) and RosarioSIS (GPL-2.0); this is its **first permissive Brazilian delivery platform**, and it is aimed at exactly the low-income-learner segment the budget finding above says Brazil actually funds. 🔴 **Node 12 is end-of-life** — the engagement opens with a runtime migration, and that belongs in the estimate rather than in week three.

##### 🆕 p681 — the leg nilled a 30th time, every UNESCO figure held, and the region gained no address

🔴 **The LATAM leg returned nothing new.** Measured against HEAD: the **UNESCO IESALC** study of **200 higher-education institutions across 19 countries** — **87%** using AI in at least one area, **74%** in teaching and learning, **26%** with a formal framework, **55%** with no AI budget, **8%** of students considering themselves fully informed of their institution's guidelines (held); **Uruguay's Ceibal** reading of **75%** of public-school teachers using AI tools (held); **fewer than 10%** of schools in the region with formal guidelines and the capacity to apply them (held); the **UNESCO Observatory on AI in Education for LATAM and the Caribbean**, launched `2026-04-14` at ECLAC in Santiago (held); CENIA, Tecnológico de Monterrey, Cetic.br (held).

🟡 **No LATAM address was placed this pass, and the reason is worth stating rather than leaving as silence.** Of `25` candidates probed, the two with a named LATAM origin in this base's backlog were already held, and the heads swept this pass — OER corpus access, workforce skills taxonomy, student wellbeing, early warning — returned **no LATAM-origin repository with a permissive grant.** 🔵 **That is consistent with `p663`'s finding and does not revise it:** LATAM supply on this base moved when the *query language* changed (Spanish-language legs), not when the topic changed. **None of p681's four new heads was run in Spanish or Portuguese** — the sweep was English-only, and the nil should be read as a limit on this pass's instrument, not on the region.

🔵 **Opportunity, placed, and the UNESCO numbers describe it precisely.** **87% adoption against 26% governance and 55% with no budget** is not a demand gap; it is a **governance-and-funding gap on top of demand that already exists**. The institutions have the usage and lack the framework, the budget line, and — per the same study — the staff who can tell students what the rules are. 🟢 **The sellable artefact is the cheapest one on this page:** an institutional AI framework with the instrumentation to show a regulator or a funder that it is being followed, built on the Observatory's own regional reference points so it reads as alignment rather than invention. 🔴 **The budget constraint is the design constraint**, which is why the offline-first, locally-hosted patterns (`P47`, `P52`) matter more here than anywhere else: `55%` with no AI budget cannot absorb a per-seat frontier-model bill, and a pattern that cannot run on institution-owned hardware cannot be sold into that number.

🟢 **p679 reading — the adoption/governance gap is now measured, large, and it *is* the engagement.** UNESCO IESALC surveyed ~**200 higher-education institutions across 19 countries** and found the single most useful number in this whole section:
- **87%** of institutions use AI in at least one area — but only **26%** have **any** formal framework.
- **55%** of universities have **no specific AI budget**; among public universities, only **4%** have dedicated AI funding. Adoption is largely **bottom-up**, driven by individual academics, researchers and students rather than institutional strategy.
- **Uruguay:** **75%** of public-school teachers use AI tools (Ceibal, 2026) — the highest measured teacher adoption in the region — while only **37%** say they conceptually understand the tool.
- **Brazil:** **53%** of school administrators use AI, but only **22%** say their school has guidelines (Cetic.br, 2026).
- **Institutions to engage:** UNESCO's **Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean** launched **2026-04-14** in Santiago, hosted by **ECLAC**; **CENIA** (Chile) partners with UNESCO on ethical AI in education; **Tecnológico de Monterrey** signed a UNESCO agreement in **March 2026**; Ceibal runs the **EduIA Lab** regional laboratory with the World Bank and the IDB.
- **Extraterritorial pressure:** LATAM vendors selling into the EU inherit the AI Act, and may be **excluded from tenders and global supply chains** without conformity evidence — which converts EU compliance from a cost into a market-access argument.

🔵 **Opportunity, placed — LATAM is the one region where this base holds a complete curriculum stack.** The **`bncc.dev`** family covers all three layers for Brazil's national curriculum: [`bncc-dados`](https://github.com/bncc-dev/bncc-dados) (MIT + CC BY 4.0, **1,721** learning objectives — 1,580 across the three stages of basic education plus **141** from the Computing complement, *Parecer CNE/CEB 2/2022* — each with competencies, organisation context and provenance, checked against the official document, DOI `10.5281/zenodo.21625233`), [`bncc-pacotes`](https://github.com/bncc-dev/bncc-pacotes) (**MCP server** via `npx -y @bncc/mcp`, plus npm and PyPI packages, hosted MCP at `mcp.bncc.dev` over streamable HTTP with **no API key**), and now [`bncc-benchmark`](https://github.com/bncc-dev/bncc-benchmark) (**the measurement layer**: 19 models × 900 responses, faithful-text rate **88% → 3%**, private held-out set). **Grounding *and* proof of grounding, for a national curriculum, under permissive terms.** The 87%/26% gap means the governance wrapper sells before the tutor does — and the benchmark is what makes the governance claim testable rather than rhetorical. 🔴 **Compliance note:** `bncc-pacotes`' licence explicitly excludes the **name and visual identity** from the grant; a Globant derivative must not present itself as `bncc.dev`.

🔴 **Declared gap, unchanged and worth restating because it is counter-intuitive.** The region's most-measured adoption — Uruguay's **75%** of teachers — has **no open-source artefact behind it**. Ceibal/ANEP released five GenAI tools on **2026-06-16** (three for teachers, two for students), but **CREA**, the LMS they sit in, is **PowerSchool-based and closed**, and no public Ceibal source repository was found this pass. The highest-adoption education system in LATAM is therefore **not** a reusable starting point, and any Uruguay engagement is integration against a closed platform, not adoption of an open one.
