| **HolonIQ** (via a vendor blog) | **`$12.3B`** (2026) | — |
| **Azumo** (vendor) | **`$136.79B`** by 2035 | 9 yr |

🟡 **The two `2026` prints — `$11.40B` and `$12.3B` — agree to within `8%`, which is this series' best agreement to date.** 🔴 **The horizons do not reconcile:** `$57.25B` by 2033 and `$136.79B` by 2035 cannot both describe the same market unless the second counts a materially wider scope. **Use the `2026` figure with both prints cited, and refuse the long-horizon numbers** — the same discipline `695` imposed after finding one source counted twice.

🟢 **Adoption is past the question of whether, on the practitioner side**: **`83%`** of K-12 teachers reported using generative-AI tools and **`93%`** of higher-education staff expect to expand AI use over the next two years (vendor-compiled, treat as directional). Compare LATAM's **`92%` student / `79%` faculty** engagement above: **the practitioner-adoption question is closed in every region this page can measure, and the governance question is open in all of them.**

🟢 **The structural trend across all five regions this pass: from experimentation to GOVERNED OPERATION.** HolonIQ expects selective acceleration tied to proven instructional value; 1EdTech expects comprehensive institutional AI strategies with real oversight; Campus Technology's and THE Journal's panels expect AI **embedded** in admissions, advising, accessibility and administration rather than run as pilots. 🟢 **The OECD's 2026 Digital Education Outlook recommends purpose-built educational AI over general-purpose tools** — which is the single best external citation this base has for why a vertical KB like this one exists, and for why a studio should not hand a university a general chatbot. 1EdTech additionally expects **digital credentials and skills-based pathways** to rise as employer expectations shift.

It is to publish the caveat wherever the counts are published, as here.

A `25`-row audit of the inherited shelf returned **`25/25` resolved, `21` exact, `4` drifted upward `+2…+6`, `0` wrong** (`711-D`). 🔴 **And the counter-instrument warning: secondary blogs are NOT a star channel** — this pass's trending leg quoted [`HKUDS/DeepTutor`](https://github.com/HKUDS/DeepTutor) at *“45K”* against an index value of **`56,881`**, understating it by roughly **`12,000`** stars.

#### 🟢 p709 — Global: what the `18`-row cohort says about every region, plus two instrument warnings that belong on THIS page

🟢 **(1) The regional census moved, and the direction is the readable part.** `EMEA 18 → 22`, `APAC 52 → 56`, `Global 83 → 89`, **`North America 20` and `LATAM 19` unchanged.** The whole of the placed movement came from **institution-bound** repositories — servers named after one university's own portal, catalogue or syllabus system — which means **this base's ability to place a row by region is now largely a function of whether a region's institutions OPERATE their own systems or BUY them.** Japan and Korea operate; North America buys; EMEA is mixed; LATAM's institution-bound open-source layer is empty (`709-E`).

🔴 **(2) The demand legs nil for the 48th consecutive pass, and p709 measured the nil instead of asserting it.** Each marker the four regional legs returned was grepped against this base's own `intel/` before anything was written: `14` markers checked, **`13` already held with `24`–`206` occurrences each, `1` absent** — and the absent one (`Income Group`) is a World Bank data-grouping field from a repository README, **not a regional fact at all.** 🟡 **The useful conclusion is about the instrument, not the market: these four legs have reached saturation against this base, and a pass that reports them as “researched” without measuring overlap is reporting effort, not information.**

🔴 **(3) Instrument warning that belongs on an intel page: `NOASSERTION` is not “unknown, probably permissive”.** [`balejosg/ClassroomPath`](https://github.com/balejosg/ClassroomPath) reports `spdx_id` `NOASSERTION`; its `4,137 B` payload is a bespoke **“Source-Available License 1.0”** that forbids all `Production Use`, **explicitly including “pilots” and “internal institutional rollouts”**. A market map keyed on `spdx_id` would carry that as missing metadata rather than as a prohibition. **Any opportunity on this page that names a platform must be read against the platform's payload, not its badge.**

🔴 **(4) Instrument warning, second: a README's licence claim about a sibling is a claim, not a grant.** [`balejosg/normativa-madrid`](https://github.com/balejosg/normativa-madrid) credits an MIT ancestor that **serves `404` for all five grant filenames** while being alive (`709-G`). **The opportunity in `normativa-madrid` is real; one code path in it is not yet redistributable.**


#### 🟢 p708 — Global: what the institutional-agent cohort says about EVERY region, and the one honest instrument warning that belongs on this page

🔵 **p708's mandated market and trends legs nilled for the 47th consecutive time, measured against `18` named markers that were each grepped against the live shelves — `18 / 18` already held** (the full table is `708-B` in [`../agents/trending.md`](../agents/trending.md)). 🟢 **The `2026` bracket on this page therefore survives a seventh independent check unchanged: `$11.40B`–`$12.3B` for `2026`**, with Coherent's `$57.25B by 2033` implying `25.93%` — arithmetic this page already publishes and p708 recomputed rather than copied.

🟢 **The region-independent finding of the pass: the reusable asset in education AI is the AUTHORITY LAYER, not the model, and it now exists in permissively licensed code.** p708's seven filed subjects are institutional agents from four countries, built independently by different authors, and they converge on the same four controls:

| control | where it is implemented, permissively | why a client's gate asks for it |
|---|---|---|
| **Role-aware scope enforced server-side**, not trusted to the client | [`changazo/udesa-mcp`](https://github.com/changazo/udesa-mcp) — *estudiante* / *docente* / *gestor* | a student agent must not be able to request another student's record at all |
| **Compulsory preview-and-approve before any write**, with separate READ and WRITE registries | [`EduardoKrausME/moodle-local_mcp`](https://github.com/EduardoKrausME/moodle-local_mcp) 🔴 GPL-3.0 — read as a **specification** | an agent that posts to a forum or submits an assignment is acting as the user |
| **Pseudonymisation before anything reaches a model** — students arrive under an alias | [`falkenslab/miyagi`](https://github.com/falkenslab/miyagi) 🟢 MIT | California's **`AB 1159`** bars student data from model training; the EU AI Act classifies education as high-risk |
| **Provenance, coverage and warnings on every result**; an error is never returned as an empty result | `changazo/udesa-mcp` | a tutor that silently returns nothing is indistinguishable from one that returns the truth |

🔵 **Credentials never leave the user in any of them** — OS secure store, or the institution's own session, or private single-account OAuth. 🟢 **None of these four is a research problem. All four are shelved, readable and permissively licensed today**, and three of the four are under MIT or Apache-2.0 with no copyleft to negotiate. 🔴 **The fourth, the write-authority model, has its best reference implementation under GPL-3.0 — by construction, because it lives inside Moodle's tree — so it must be re-implemented out-of-tree rather than copied.** That boundary is drawn in full at [`../verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) `708-V1`.

🔴 **The instrument warning, and it applies to every figure and every URL on this page.** Under this session's network policy `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` returns **`403`**, `curl -sI https://github.com/{owner}/{repo}` returns **`403`** for repositories `git ls-remote --symref` proves ALIVE, and `img.shields.io` is unreachable (`http_code 000`). 🟢 **What works: `git ls-remote --symref` for existence and default ref, `raw.githubusercontent.com` for the grant payload bytes, `git clone --depth 1 --bare` for commit date and tree size.** 🔴 **No star count was obtainable at p708 at all** — the `Stars` cells on this pass's eight rows read `—` rather than a guess, and no existing star cell was touched. 🔵 **A pass that mistook `github.com`'s `403` for a `404` would have refused all eight live subjects and could have retired `192` good agent rows and `267` good foundations; on this network a `403` from `github.com` is evidence about the proxy, never about the repository.**

#### 🟢 p706 — Global: what is true in every region, and the two things that are not region-specific at all

🟢 **Two constraints recurred in EVERY regional leg this pass, which makes them global requirements rather than regional colour:**

1. **Student-data custody is the live adoption brake everywhere** — named as COPPA in North America, as AI Act post-market surveillance and “European LLMs” in EMEA, as sovereignty in APAC, and as ministry-level caution in LATAM. 🔵 **The cheapest architectural answer this pass found is BYOK** — [`its-ahoh/litcode`](https://github.com/its-ahoh/litcode) moves model cost **and** data custody to the learner — with **local-first** and **read-only** integration ([`EdmundD28/moodle-codex`](https://github.com/EdmundD28/moodle-codex)) as the institutional variants.
2. **The system of record, not the model, is the hard part.** Every high-value placement this pass is an agent layer over a system an institution already runs — Wilma, Aula, Moodle, UnB, SLIIT. 🔵 **A studio's reusable asset is the connector, not the prompt.**

🔴 **Market size — unchanged in substance from p705 and restated as a warning:** the `2026` AI-in-education figures returned this pass (~**`$7.5B`** `2025` → ~**`$10.6B`** `2026`, CAGR ~**`40.9%`**) come from **paid vendor reports whose methodology cannot be inspected**, and this base has already documented (`695`) a case of *“three sources, three CAGRs”* resolving to **one source counted twice**. **Treat every figure on this page as a vendor's model, and prefer dated appropriations and funded programmes** — `$169M` US DoE, `$4.6M` Google.org, `$4.6m` Raspberry Pi Foundation — **which are facts with dates attached.**

🟢 **Cross-regional readiness, single instrument (Workday), so the comparison is legitimate:** **EMEA `56%` · Americas `48%` · APJ `45%`** self-reported AI preparedness. 🔵 **Note the inversion worth carrying into a pitch: EMEA reports the highest readiness and carries the heaviest regulation, while APAC reports the lowest readiness and has this base's deepest open-source education supply.**

#### 🟢 p704 — Global: the asset shortage in education AI is not models or repos, it is **readable licences**

🔵 **`p704`'s cross-regional finding came from reading grant files rather than market copy, and it reframes what a studio sells first.** Nineteen subjects were probed at the payload this pass. **Four failed, and each failed differently:**

| Failure shape | Instance | Why it bites a client |
|---|---|---|
| **Source-available masquerading as open source** | [`canyongbs/advisingapp`](https://github.com/canyongbs/advisingapp), `340★`, **Elastic 2.0** | The licence's named prohibition — *“hosted or managed service”* — **is** the studio delivery model |
| **A permissive claim that is simply false** | [`idurar/idurar-erp-crm`](https://github.com/idurar/idurar-erp-crm), `8,944★`, **AGPL-3.0** (a mandated leg called it MIT) | AGPL's network clause reaches the portal's end users |
| **A grant reference that dangles** | [`DMontgomery40/mcp-canvas-lms`](https://github.com/DMontgomery40/mcp-canvas-lms), `104★`, README says MIT, **`10` paths all `404`** | A README grep returns "MIT"; there is no grant at all |
| **A notice with no holder** | [`langx/langx`](https://github.com/langx/langx), `109★`, **BSD-3-Clause**, *“Copyright (c) 2024”* and no name | Symmetric: binds no one, and leaves no one to ask |

🟢 **All four were found with `curl` against `raw.githubusercontent.com` and one batched `gh api search/repositories` call.** Two of the four are **top-decile adopted** in their niches. 🔵 **The generalisation:** in education specifically, "open source" is being asserted by README, by blog roundup and by star count far more often than it is established by a grant file — and the education buyer (a university procurement office, a ministry) is the buyer least equipped to tell the difference and **most** exposed when it is wrong. **A licence-gate pass is therefore the cheapest, earliest, most defensible thing a studio can sell into an education engagement**, and it is specified as `P96`.

🟢 **The second global finding is internal and it changes how this base should be worked.** `12` of `p704`'s `13` verified subjects were already written in [`../agents/trending.md`](../agents/trending.md) and present in **no** live shelf (`704-B`). The mandated legs produced `18` addresses and **`18 / 18` were already held**. **This base's constraint is promotion, not discovery** — a `grep -rlF` against its own backlog outperformed eight web legs by `12` rows to `0`.

🔴 **And one global limit is now named rather than rediscovered:** stars for any repository not attached to the session are available **only** through `gh api search/repositories`; `github.com` HTML returns a `378`-byte interception stub and `api.github.com/repos/…` returns `403` (`704-C`). A repository the search index omits therefore has **no star channel at all** — `704-D` is that case. **Not a transient failure; retrying will not fix it.**


#### 🟢 p703 — Global: **`3` new agent rows, and all three are MECHANISMS rather than platforms — which is where the global gap actually is**

🟢 **Global went `67 / 144` → `70 / 158`, and the three additions share a property worth naming: none of them is a product, and each encodes a piece of pedagogy as configuration.**

- [`VIABLE-Learning-Lab/viable`](https://github.com/VIABLE-Learning-Lab/viable) (MIT code, **CC BY 4.0 data** in `NOTICE`) — a **Harness** that makes any agent read the teaching moment (the goal, how much help is right today, the one misconception that fits) before answering, plus an **Engine** that runs classes on a school's own server against any model. 🔵 **The first artefact on these shelves whose unit of configuration is the teacher's help limit rather than the model**, and a split grant whose data obligation the code licence never mentions.
- [`dylanj7/scholara`](https://github.com/dylanj7/scholara) (MIT) — a tutor built as a **refusal boundary**: it will build an outline skeleton and probe a thesis, and will not write prose a student could paste or compute a maths step, declared per subject. Its author names the real problem precisely — *the hard part of an AI tutor is not the chat interface, it is the refusal boundary* — and published a security review of their own MVP's holes.
- [`NiravRVaghasiya/Polyglot-Swarm`](https://github.com/NiravRVaghasiya/Polyglot-Swarm) (MIT) — a persisted **learner model** (per-skill competence) driving an **FSRS** scheduler driving a conversational tutor: the three primitives this base keeps naming separately, wired into one loop, running fully locally.

🔵 **The global structural reading: model-layer and framework supply is abundant and already held here, so the scarce artefact is pedagogy encoded as something a machine can act on.** `44` rows filed this pass and the ones that will still matter in a year are the ones that answer *how much help, and when* — not the ones that answer *which model*.

**Demand side, with disagreement preserved rather than resolved.** 🔴 **Market-size estimates for AI in education do not agree and this base will not pick one**: one report gives **~$7.52B (2025) → $10.6B (2026) at a 40.9% CAGR**, another frames 2026 as a **$32B** market. Both are secondary; the methodologies have not been seen. What the sources agree on is directional: agentic AI named as 2026's transformation peak, predictive analytics for student retention, AI in institutional operations and administration, K-12 early intervention, course design compressing from months to days — and, as a counterweight, a reported **"human-centric" pivot** with reinvestment in social-emotional learning as AI absorbs more cognitive tasks, plus a flagged **crisis of explainability** as AI takes over curriculum design. 🟡 **Caveat on the whole demand picture, stated: many of these trend pieces are auto-generated posts repackaging the same few primary sources (Deloitte, EdSurge, eSchool News), and this base has read the repackaging, not the primaries.**

**Opportunities.** (1) 🟢 **Compose, don't build.** The highest-value thing p703 found anywhere is [`malkreide/socratic-method-skill`](https://github.com/malkreide/socratic-method-skill) at `55★` — a German-language agent skill, no server, no database, no model choice, droppable into an engagement in an afternoon. **Skills are the cheapest composable unit on these shelves and this base holds almost none.** (2) 🟢 **Explainability is a named concern and `P91` is the answer to it** — floor, repeated-run consistency, agreement with human markers, with no pass threshold so a failure stays visible. (3) **Corpus is solved and should stop being treated as a cost**: [`cyanheads/gutenberg-mcp-server`](https://github.com/cyanheads/gutenberg-mcp-server) puts `78,000+` public-domain full texts behind one tool, so a derivative tutor can quote them in full with no licence negotiation. (4) 🔵 **Sell the transcribe/judge split (`T149`) as the default assessment architecture** — three teams in three languages converged on it independently this pass, which is stronger evidence than any vendor roadmap.

#### 🟢 p700 — Global: the eight mandated legs nil a **32nd** consecutive time with a denominator of `20`, and the pass's transferable product is a **channel**, not a repository

🔴 **The legs, scored.** All eight mandated searches ran (four global, four regional). **`20` checkable facts: `20` already held, `0` candidate-novel, `0` adoptable.** Seven addresses surfaced and **all seven were already shelved** — `GarethManning/education-agent-skills`, `OPTIMETA/PAIDEIA`, `microsoft/ai-agents-for-beginners`, `rohitg00/ai-engineering-from-scratch`, `TapXWorld/ChinaTextbook`, `speedyapply/2026-AI-College-Jobs`, `openeducat/openeducat_erp`. The global legs returned aggregator and SEO content almost exclusively; the ERP leg could not establish OpenEduCat's licence, which this base has held for many passes.

🟢 **The pass's product is `T134`: every row this base files carries an owner it has never queried.** Found by a gate, not by reasoning — a duplicate-slug check rejected `eemtijca/buscapp` as already filed at `p693`, which exposed that the *school* was known while the *organisation* had never been asked what else it publishes. Across eleven already-filed owners: **`356` siblings → `337` novel → `159` permissive, live, pushed in 2026 → `20` education-shaped → `6` filed.** A `30%` filing rate on the education-shaped subset, against a topic channel `680-B` measured at `1/21` adoptable and declared finished.

🟢 **Why the owner channel is structurally sounder than the topic channel.** A topic head is a stranger's tagging decision — which is why `692-H` retired four heads as *collided* and `T105` had to be written. An owner is a fact this base has **already verified**: it filed a row from it, read its grant, confirmed liveness. Expansion inherits that provenance. **Standing instruction: when a row is filed, query its owner in the same pass.**

🔵 **Two collided heads confirmed in the `692-H` sense, so a later pass does not re-mine them.** `topic:exam` (`1,752`) is overwhelmingly **IT-certification cram** — DCA, OSCP, CKA, CKAD, ExamTopics scrapers. `topic:academic` (`3,056`) is **researcher personal tooling** — Zotero bridges, LaTeX templates, academic CV themes, paper readers. Both return real stars, real permissive licences and real 2026 activity, which is exactly what makes them dangerous: a licence-and-liveness pipeline will promote a stranger's Hugo CV theme into an education KB.

🟢 **A permissive research-integrity layer confirmed on a second, independent channel.** `692-C` found it via `topic:peer-review`; p700 reached the same layer through `topic:literature-review` and `topic:academic` and filed its two strongest instruments: [`Altman-conquer/bibtex-verifier`](https://github.com/Altman-conquer/bibtex-verifier) (MIT, `142★`) — which checks citations against **OpenAlex and CrossRef** and is therefore **decidable rather than probabilistic**, the thing `T79` said the sector lacked — and [`openjournals/joss`](https://github.com/openjournals/joss) (MIT, `1,934★`), the running machinery of a real GitHub-native peer-reviewed journal.

🔴 **Three defects in p700's own instruments, all caught by controls before publication, all published as findings.** (1) The `ls-remote --symref` parser **truncated a slash-containing default branch** (`chore/bump-1.1.6` → `chore`), and the failure arrives disguised as a licence finding because the truncated ref then `404`s — `T131`'s sibling, registered as `700-A`. (2) The grant probe was not **case**-exhaustive and would have refused a `1,230★` Apache-2.0 corpus — `T131`. (3) A sibling was nearly double-filed; the duplicate-slug gate caught it and the catch became `T134`.

🔵 **Channel map, re-measured.** `search/repositories` and `raw.githubusercontent.com` live. `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` and **`users/{owner}` both `403`** under this session's repository scope, and `search/users` **does not expose `location`** — so **owner-location placement is unavailable on the API channel this pass** (`700-G`). Region placement therefore came only from grant payloads, repository descriptions and READMEs, and two rows were deliberately left `Global` rather than guessed.

**Where the money is, globally, this pass:** the sellable artefact is a **grounding-and-refusal guarantee with a gain measure attached** — `P84` — because it satisfies a regulated buyer and an offline buyer with the same parts. The sellable *method*, for this base itself, is `T134`.


#### 🔵 p699 — Global: the four mandated global legs are **saturated past the point of usefulness**, and the pass's global finding is an instrument correction

🔴 **The four mandated global legs returned nothing filable, measured rather than asserted.** `top open source AI agents education 2026 github MIT`, `github trending education AI 2026`, `open source platform education ERP CRM MIT Apache` and `AI education industry trends 2026` returned, between them: general-purpose agent rankings (AutoGPT, LangChain, OpenDevin) with **no education-specific MIT list**, third-party trending aggregators rather than GitHub itself, **no education-specific MIT or Apache ERP/CRM** (the permissive hits were horizontal — Krayin CRM, Huly; OpenEduCat is Odoo-native and already shelved; ERPNext is GPL-3.0), and market-research listings. 🔴 **Several results were visibly auto-generated SEO pages**, which is `T118`'s problem (a star floor stops filtering noise once the noise is star-farmed) arriving in the *web* channel rather than the GitHub one.

🔴 **The agent-shaped legs reproduce a specific, now twice-measured pathology: they return *tutorials about AI* instead of *AI for education*.** Five sweeps with star floors and push-recency filters (`education AI agent`, `AI tutor agent`, `topic:ai-education`, `lesson plan generator AI`, `automated grading AI`) surfaced, as their highest-ranked novel permissive results, an *AI 30-day learning challenge*, a *DeepSeek agent tutorial*, a *bilingual Claude-Code/Codex tutorial series*, an enterprise **RAG** platform and an AI **evaluation SDK**. **The English token `tutor`/`learning`/`education` is read by GitHub's index as "material that teaches AI", not "software that teaches people"** — the same homograph class as `T105` and `T124`, now confirmed on the agent vocabulary specifically. One leg (`lesson plan generator AI`) returned **zero** permissive novel results at all.

🟢 **`1` agent row was filed from `60`-plus novel addresses, and `1` was refused** — see [`../agents/trending.md`](../agents/trending.md) `699-C`/`699-D`. `T116` holds: this base's binding constraint is filing, not discovery.

🟢 **The global product of the pass is three instrument findings, which transfer to every region:** `T127` (a grant digest is a provenance signal for MIT and a non-signal for Apache-2.0, controlled at both signs), `T128` (a licence-uniform estate clears in bulk, and the digest partition recovers undocumented holder history), `T129` (`org:` is an existence oracle for an owner; the mandated `curl -sI` is an oracle for nothing, re-controlled at four signs). 🟢 **And one gate result: `make hooks` RAN at p699** after six passes of denial — `47 / 71` green on entry, and p699 closed two of the reds by repairing defects in **what these pages publish**: a shelf table split in two by a stray blank line, and `46` out-of-vocabulary region headings on *this page*. `699-E`.


#### 🟢 p694 — Global: the shelves gained their **first complete permissive chains**, which is a different kind of asset from a row

🟢 **Three chains closed at p694, each one previously broken by a copyleft link** (`T112`):

| Chain | Links | Previously blocked by |
|---|---|---|
| **Learning analytics** | [`xapipe`](https://github.com/yetanalytics/xapipe) (Apache-2.0, graded verbatim) → [`warren`](https://github.com/openfun/warren) (MIT) → [`tna`](https://github.com/sonsoleslp/tna) (MIT) | `learnhouse` **AGPL-3.0**, `oat-sa/lib-lti1p3-core` **GPL-2.0** |
| **Retention** | [`py-fsrs`](https://github.com/open-spaced-repetition/py-fsrs) (MIT, `524★`) beside this base's knowledge-tracing rows | Anki **AGPL-3.0** — same algorithm, copyleft tree |
| **Credentials** | [`euron-open-lms`](https://github.com/euron-sde-team/euron-open-lms) issues (MIT) → [`learner-credential-wallet`](https://github.com/digitalcredentials/learner-credential-wallet) holds (MIT) | nothing permissive on the holder side at all |

🔵 **Why this is recorded as a Global opportunity rather than as three rows.** `694` passes have measured this base by row count. **A client deliverable is not blocked by a missing row; it is blocked by a missing *link*** — one copyleft component makes the whole delivery copyleft, and the shelf can look rich while no usable path exists. **The rows that closed these chains have `524`, `17`, `13`, `12` and `3` stars.** None would survive a ranking by traction, and each one changed what can actually be shipped.

🟢 **The global governance opportunity, stated once and applicable in every region.** The `2026` trend consensus this base holds from four independent legs is **governance over novelty** — the OECD's `2026` Digital Education Outlook recommending purpose-built educational AI over general-purpose tools, 1EdTech's reframing from *whether* to *under what conditions*, `83 %` of K-12 teachers already using generative AI against districts with no policy, `93 %` of higher-education staff expecting to expand use. 🔵 **Every one of those is a demand signal for records and oversight rather than for capability**, and p694 filed the first permissive runtime that produces them ([`microsoft/agent-governance-toolkit`](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-governance-toolkit), MIT, `6,535★`) together with the pattern that assembles it (**`P74`**).

🔴 **And the global gap this base should stop treating as its own failure.** Education-AI **evaluation** has no permissive supply: `4` of the `5` harnesses probed at p694 ship **no licence**, including an **EMNLP 2025 Oral** and an **AAAI 2025 Oral**, and the sole adoptable one ([`AI-for-Education/pedagogy-benchmark`](https://github.com/AI-for-Education/pedagogy-benchmark), MIT) has **`13` stars**. 🔵 **The mechanism is publication, not obscurity: this work ships as papers, and papers release code as an artefact of peer review rather than as a product.** 🟢 **The consequence is a real commercial opening** — *"is this tutor pedagogically good?"* is the question an education buyer asks and the one the open ecosystem cannot currently answer, so a studio that builds a defensible pedagogical eval harness is building something the field does not have at any licence.


#### 🔴 p693 — Global: the four mandated global legs nil a 32nd time, and the licence-truth deliverable gains a fourth specimen class

🔴 **`22` checkable facts across the four global legs, `22` already held, `0` candidate-novel.** The agent leg returns general-purpose frameworks (AutoGPT, LangChain, OpenHands, OpenClaw) and **no education ranking exists on it at all**; the trending leg returns aggregator blogs rather than GitHub's own view; the ERP/CRM leg returns OpenEduCat-on-Odoo, ERPNext (GPL-3.0) and two permissive CRMs that are **not education** (Krayin MIT, Huly Apache-2.0) — reproducing this base's standing finding that **the permissive education-ERP slot is occupied only via Odoo's licence, not independently of it.**

🟢 **A fourth specimen class for the licence-truth pitch, and this one is about *identity* rather than about a false claim.** `693-G` graded four fresh Apache-2.0 payloads whose byte residuals against canonical are **`−593`, `+13`, `0`, `0`** — and **all four normalise to `8,967` characters against `apache.org`'s own text, verbatim §1–§9.** 🔴 **One of them, [`DanielDev87/virtual-center-app`](https://github.com/DanielDev87/virtual-center-app), is `11,358 B` — identical in length to canonical — with a *different* sha256.** **Same length, different hash, identical operative terms.** 🔵 **The sales line:** every automated licence check a client already runs compares a hash or a size. Both answer this pair wrongly and in opposite directions. **Only the normalised operative body answers it**, and that is a ten-line instrument this base runs with controls at both signs — reproducing `692-G2` independently, one pass later, on four new payloads.

🔴 **The ceiling on every regulatory claim on this page is environmental and should be read as a line item, not a caveat.** Six primary domains were tested at p693 and **all six fail to resolve**: `unesco.org`, `iesalc.unesco.org`, `ipsos.com`, `teqsa.gov.au`, `eur-lex.europa.eu`, `joinup.ec.europa.eu` — joining `zenodo.org`, `commonlit.org`, `sciencedirect.com` and `cambridge.org` from p665/p675. **This base can read GitHub and web-search summaries; it cannot read the primary text of European law, UN agencies, national regulators or academic publishing.** Allowing those hosts would convert a long list of this page's load-bearing secondary citations into primary ones, and it is the single cheapest improvement available to this KB.


#### 🔴 p692 — Global: the market figures did not move, and the pass's global finding is that the fastest-growing education repository of the window is not open source

**The figures, unchanged and still unconverged.** The spread this base has tracked since p664 is intact: Coherent Market Insights at `USD 11.40 Bn` (`2026`) → `57.25 Bn` (`2033`); HolonIQ at `$12.3B` for `2026`; a `$136.79 Bn`-by-`2035` outlier. **`0` novel series.** The qualitative consensus is also unchanged and is now stated the same way by every source reached: `2026` is the year education AI moves **from experimentation to governance** — purpose-built tools with safeguards over generic chatbots, and the adoption/training gap (`83%` of K-12 teachers using generative AI, `85%` feeling unprepared to manage it) as the recurring complaint.

🔴 **The global finding worth acting on.** [`chadyi/StudyOnTV`](https://github.com/chadyi/StudyOnTV) — a parental screen-time gate where a child unlocks device time by answering questions, on Android phone, tablet and TV, with an offline LaTeX-capable question bank and handwriting and voice input — took **`833★` and `199` forks in six days**, the steepest trajectory of any education repository in this window by a wide margin. **Its `LICENSE` is a `1,043 B` “Source Available License — Personal Use Only” (sha256₁₂ `67ae8445017e`, © *2024 chadyi*, “All rights reserved”). It is not open source, and a studio cannot build on it.**

🔴 **The part that is a warning rather than a disappointment: the repository's own GitHub description calls it an “open-source Android TV app”.** `681-C` found *search legs* returning false permissive claims; this is **the repository itself** making the claim in a structured metadata field, at `833★`, while its grant says the opposite one click away. `NOASSERTION` on `license.spdx_id` was the only machine-readable hint, and `NOASSERTION` is uninformative in both directions (`681-B`) — **so the only instrument that answers it is reading the payload, which is what this base does and what every list that recommended it did not.**

🔵 **The demand signal inside the decline is real and transferable.** `199` forks in six days on a *parental-control-plus-question-bank* appliance says the unserved buyer is the **household**, not the institution — and the feature set (offline question bank, no self-hosted server, star-rated time rewards, TV remote input, a mode that only gates during set hours so an elderly relative can watch TV in the daytime) is a product specification written by someone who has met the problem. **A permissive equivalent does not exist on these shelves.** That is a build opportunity, not a procurement one.


#### 🔵 p691 — Global: discovery nilled on two independent channels, `32 / 32` candidates already shelved

##### 🆕 p681 — the global legs held on market size, and the one new global finding is a licence shape rather than a number

🔴 **No new market series.** The trends leg re-served **Coherent Market Insights at `USD 11.40B` (2026) → `USD 57.25B` (2033), CAGR `16.8%`** and **HolonIQ at `$12.3B` (2026)** — both already held, and both inside the spread this page has recorded as widening rather than converging since `p664`. **The spread remains the finding.** The qualitative legs were also held: pilots-to-infrastructure, governance-as-buying-trigger, purpose-built over general-purpose tools (OECD *Digital Education Outlook 2026*), teacher workload, and skills-based pathways with verified credentials.

🟢 **The one genuinely new global finding is structural and it is about licences.** The OER layer separates cleanly into a **copyleft platform tier** and a **permissive client tier**: [`pressbooks/pressbooks`](https://github.com/pressbooks/pressbooks) is GPL-3.0 and [`PreTeXtBook/pretext`](https://github.com/PreTeXtBook/pretext) is GPL-2-or-3, while [`johnnyrobot/pressbooks-cli`](https://github.com/johnnyrobot/pressbooks-cli) reaches **28 networks holding `9,601` open textbooks under MIT, with no account, key or token**, because it is a client over a public REST API and holds no platform code. 🔵 **Generalised: when a copyleft education platform fronts a public API over an open corpus, the permissive opportunity is the client, and you need no relief from the platform's licence because you never link against it.** That argument is reusable against every GPL/AGPL platform on `verticals/solutions.md` that exposes an API — see `repos/trending.md` `681-G2` and `compose/patterns.md` **P56**.

🔴 **Beside it, a measured global nil: no MCP server or agent bridge exists for LibreTexts, OpenStax, the Open Textbook Library or OER Commons.** All four probed, all four nil. The corpora are public and openly licensed, and the access layer is missing — a gap whose hardest part is already solved under MIT.

## 🔴 `681-O` — a defect in how this base repairs itself, found twice in one pass

*(Closes the `## Opportunities by region` block above. It is deliberately **not** a `###` under that heading: the region subsections are a closed set of five — North America, EMEA, APAC, LATAM, Global — and a sixth sibling would read as a sixth region bucket to anything parsing this file.)*

| the declaration | where it was made | what the target file actually contained at `p681` |
|---|---|---|
| *"`intel/market.md`'s North America block names the claim, states that it does not verify, and points here"* | `intel/measured-unusable.md` **`651-A`** | 🔴 the North Carolina bullet asserted all three dates as fact; **`651-A` appeared nowhere in `intel/market.md`** |
| *"FILED FOR REAL THIS PASS → T45"*, of `microsoft/generative-ai-for-beginners` | `agents/trending.md` **`665-A`** | 🔴 **`123,056★`, MIT, on no shelf** — one mention, inside another row's prose |

🔵 **One shape, and it is not carelessness.** Both defects were found by a pass reading one file, recorded in **that** file, and declared as repairs to a **different** file — and in both cases the second edit never happened. **The finding and the fix live in different files, the pass is scored on the finding, and nothing re-reads the target.** 🟢 **Both are repaired at p681**: the North Carolina bullet above now carries the falsification and the pointer, and `generative-ai-for-beginners` is a filed row on `repos/foundations.md`. 🔵 **The cheap guard, recommended rather than built:** when an entry's text claims another file says something, grep that file for the claim before the pass closes. Both of these would have been caught by one `grep` — `651-A` for the string `651-A` in `intel/market.md`, `665-A` for the repository name in the shelf it named. **`P50`'s filing gate checks rows added; it cannot see a row that was never added, or a caveat that was never written.**

#### 🆕 p678 — what a studio can take from this pass regardless of region

🔵 **All nine addresses filed this pass are `Global (unplaced)`**, and that is a measured verdict rather than a default: `8` of `8` owner accounts have no `location` set, and `7` READMEs plus every grant payload named no jurisdiction (`678-I`, with both evidence tables linked there). **Unplaced supply is still usable supply** — what it cannot serve is a brief that asks "what is happening in my region".

🟢 **Three findings that travel:**

1. **A platform's licence is not its components' licence.** Open edX is `AGPL-3.0` and its two official mobile clients are `Apache-2.0`; OpenMAIC is permissive and its UI has no grant at all. **Both directions of that error appeared in one pass**, and both are invisible to a repository-level licence badge.
2. **A NOTICE is not a copyleft problem.** `basiclti-util-java` reads `13,185 B` against an `11,357 B` canonical, and the residual decomposes exactly into `1,825 B` of Apache-2.0 subcomponent attributions. A digest gate is **right to flag it and wrong to reject it** (`678-C`).
3. **Forks beat stars for institutional software.** On a 12-point out-of-sample test the `f/★ >= 0.5` boundary held: platform code (LMS clients, LTI libraries, school ERP, Kolibri installers) sat above it, agent code (`MAIC-UI` `0.212`, `wiki-rag` `0.189`) below (`678-D`). **For a platform shortlist, rank by forks per star and apply an `n >= 20` floor.**

🔴 **And one about this base itself:** p678's own intended headline — a mechanism for why two equal-length Apache-2.0 payloads hash differently — **was already held in the tree** and was caught by scoring the claim before drafting it (`678-H`). The base measures well; the standing risk is republishing its own holdings as discovery.

Any market map of the interoperability layer built on index counts will report the dormant artefact as the live one. See `repos/foundations.md` and **T59**.

🔴 **And the global caution, filed as `T55`:** across `p672` and `p673` the licence label from an index has now been wrong in **both** directions on this base — understating restrictions on a NonCommercial grant (`672-D`, fails **unsafe**) and reporting `NOASSERTION` on three independently permissive grants (`673-B`, fails by **hiding supply**). Any studio inventory that filters candidates on `license.spdx_id` is therefore **both** shipping grants it must not and discarding grants it could use. Only the byte read at the real default ref is load-bearing.

🔵 **p669 — one genuinely new document, filed as a *secondary* read and labelled as one.** **European Parliament EPRS briefing `784574`, *“Artificial Intelligence in Classrooms: Pedagogical Dimensions”*, `15` pages, author Vasiliki Charisi, requested by the CULT Committee.** Its load-bearing claim, quoted verbatim: *“A learner-centred approach requires that clearly defined educational objectives, rather than technological capabilities, guide the use of AI in classrooms.”* It focuses on **primary and secondary education**, covers the teacher's role and how pedagogical considerations can be operationalised through policy, and flags **insufficient pedagogical guidance in classroom AI tools** as a concern. 🔴 **Evidence class, stated plainly: this is a search-index read, not a byte-read of the primary PDF.** `www.europarl.europa.eu` fails at **name resolution** on two independent channels (`curl` at p668, `WebFetch` at p669), so the host is outside this session's network policy and the primary cannot be verified here. **It is filed at the lower class rather than withheld, and it must not be re-quoted as a primary read.** 🔵 **Why it matters commercially:** taken with the OECD recommendation this page already carries, it is a *second* institutional endorsement — from the EU's own research service — of purpose-built pedagogical tooling over general-purpose assistants, which is precisely what this KB's shelf is made of.

It has **no root grant file at all** (seven filenames probed, all 404), and its `package.json` declares **eleven families joined by AND, including `GPL-3.0-only` and `LGPL-3.0-only`** (**T38**). Any studio that trusted the index would have shipped GPL code inside a proprietary deliverable. This is the first address this base has found where the index errs toward *permissive* — the dangerous direction; every prior case (`NOASSERTION`, p646's `ltijs`) erred toward restrictive.

Alongside it, two `NOASSERTION` repositories resolved to usable grants on reading the payload (`microsoft/pxt-microbit` → MIT behind a bespoke two-line header; `code-dot-org/code-dot-org` → Apache-2.0 plus an enumerated media carve-out), and two Blockly derivatives were found shipping a **non-canonical Apache-2.0** payload — header misdated "January 2011", appendix truncated, byte-identical across `scratch-blocks` and `ardublockly`, operative terms intact (**T40**).

🔵 **The offer:** an open-source licence audit that reads grant payloads on the resolved default branch instead of trusting a metadata field, delivered as an SBOM a client's counsel can rely on. It is cheap to run, it is demonstrably not what tooling does by default, and the four findings above were produced in a single pass against one industry's shelf. Scope it as 1–2 weeks against an existing codebase, and lead with the eleven-family specimen.



🔵 **p667 — the global reading of this pass is about the *evidence layer*, not the market size.** The size figures on this page are unchanged and still disagree by a factor of five on the same year, which `p664` recorded and this pass reproduces rather than resolves. What changed is what a studio can put behind a retention claim. 🟢 **`repos/foundations.md` gained sixteen rows this pass that are one layer — retention and scheduling — where this base previously held a single point.** The layer now contains a peer-reviewed optimum with its dataset (`memorize`, PNAS 2019), a published alternative scheduler (`SSP-MMC`), the highest-starred permissive client at `1,870★` BSD-3 (`olmps/memo`), terminal, web and three Obsidian-native implementations, the `.apkg` writer that completes `anki-mcp-server`'s pair, two incremental-reading tools, the teacher-facing code-assessment surface the p647 assessment layer lacked, and a 1,469-question permissive item bank. 🔵 **The practical consequence for an engagement:** the conversation about whether generated learning material actually sticks used to end at "use FSRS". It can now start from an objective function, a delivery form and a citation — and three of those rows make the deck **a text file in a repository**, which is the provenance answer regulated buyers in every region on this page keep asking for.


#### 🟢 New at p668 — the 2026 consensus held under a fourth independent re-test, and the market-size spread did not narrow

- 🔴 **All four global legs and all four regional legs re-run at `2026-11-05`: `27` of `28` facts already held on this base's shelves, controls clean at both signs.** That is the **twenty-second** consecutive nil on the mandated demand-side legs. 🟢 **The consensus `T35` records — governed, purpose-built, teacher-first education AI rather than generic chatbots — is now confirmed by a fourth independent sweep** (1EdTech's "how, not whether"; HolonIQ's selective acceleration; Campus Technology's embedded-infrastructure reading; the OECD *Digital Education Outlook* recommending purpose-built educational AI over general-purpose tools).
- 🔴 **And the market-size spread is still the finding, not the number.** Coherent Market Insights now publishes **`$11.40B` (2026) → `$57.25B` (2033) at `16.8%` CAGR**. Set against the readings this page already carries, the disagreement on the *same year* persists at roughly a factor of five, and the `16.8%` CAGR is less than half the `41.5%` that earlier sources on this page assert. 🔵 **Quote the spread and the method, never a single figure** — the aggressive numbers continue to come from vendor and aggregator pages, and one widely-cited `86%`-of-students adoption figure still traces only to an aggregator with no primary source.
- 🟢 **Skills and credentials remain a named 2026 buying trend** (1EdTech: employer expectations pushing a skills-driven ecosystem with digital credentials), which is `T33`/`T34` — and `T33`'s finding that the credential-issuing layer is almost entirely copyleft with exactly one permissive member is the constraint to price in.

- 🆕 **p671 — one global finding, and it is about the toolchain rather than the market.** `T52`: **an open-source education dependency can change owner while every automated check an engagement runs keeps passing at the old address, indefinitely**, because GitHub honours a rename redirect forever and the licence bytes travel with the repository. Measured at `2` of `197` addresses, both proven at `HEAD`-sha and licence-payload level. 🔵 **The only channel that distinguishes a renamed address from a current one is the search index, and only as an error** (`422` on the repo with `≥1` on the owner — `671-J`, controlled on `18` probes at both signs). **This applies to every industry, not just education**, and is the most transferable thing this pass produced.
- 🔴 **A second global caution, aimed at this base's own house style:** `671-R` found a licence payload at **exactly** the canonical Apache-2.0 byte count whose `sha256` differs, because a prepended blank line and a missing final newline **cancel in length**. The shelves print byte counts prominently. **Hash, do not count.**
- 🔴 **Declared globally: no new open-source education supply was filable from any of the eight legs or the `52`-address census** — the fourth consecutive pass with that result, now with a measured cause (`671-E`, `671-F`) rather than an impression.
- 🔴 **p680 — a scoping consequence of the channel census, stated globally because it changes where the next pass should spend its budget.** The GitHub topic channel is **saturated**: `63.8%` of everything it returned (`51` of `80`) is already on these shelves, and of the `21` addresses absent from this repository entirely, **`1`** is a permissive, live education asset. 🔵 The channels that have actually produced supply on this base in recent passes are **org enumeration**, **held repositories' own READMEs**, and **this repository's own unfiled backlog** — two of p680's three filings came from the last of those, measured at `p154` and shelved nowhere for `526` passes. See `intel/trends.md` **T72**.
- 🔵 **p680 closes a layer question that had never been asked directly: academic integrity.** `topic:plagiarism-detection` was swept for the first time (`19` repositories) and the split is clean and sellable: the **live** tools are copyleft — [`jplag/JPlag`](https://github.com/jplag/JPlag) (`1,997★`, GPL-3.0) and [`trailofbits/vendetect`](https://github.com/trailofbits/vendetect) (`86★`, AGPL-3.0) — the **permissive** ones are dormant (`fyrestone/pycode_similar` `191★`, last pushed `2023-06-05`; `automoss/automoss` `157★`, `2023-06-11`), and there is exactly **one live permissive option**, [`dodona-edu/dolos`](https://github.com/dodona-edu/dolos) (`357★`, MIT, Ghent University), **which this base already holds.** 🔴 Say this plainly in scoping: source-code similarity detection has a single permissive answer, and a client who rejects it is choosing between GPL-3.0 and building.

## Regional comparison

| | North America | EMEA | APAC | LATAM |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Binding AI-education rules | State-level, patchy; **no Canadian AI Act (AIDA died Jan 2025)** | **Yes — EU AI Act, risk-tiered**; UK light-touch but has a DfE **product safety standard** (Jan 2026) | **Yes, in two jurisdictions — South Korea** (AI Basic Act, education named in the high-impact definition) **and Vietnam** (Decision 33/2026/QD-TTg: education is 3 of 46 high-risk systems) | Mostly pending (Peru only) |
| Nearest enforcement date | No single federal date; state deadlines vary and several 2026 claims do not verify (see above). Canada: none | **High-risk 2 Dec 2027**; Article 50 transparency live since 2 Aug 2026 | **Korea administrative fines ≈ Jan 2027**; **Vietnam education transition 1 Sep 2027**, high-risk list live since 15 Aug 2026; India labelling live since 20 Feb 2026 | — |
| Market size (2026, directional) | largest | Europe ≈ $2.64B; MEA ≈ $0.56B | ≈ $2.85B, **35.3% CAGR — fastest** | growing; **55% of universities have no AI budget** |
| Institutional readiness | Moderate; 60% of teachers use AI, 55% of schools unrestricted | Moderate–high; ~70% have or are developing guidance | Mixed (China, Singapore have policies; much of the region does not) | **Low — 26% have a strategy, <10% have guidelines** |
| Dominant LMS | Canvas, Blackboard | Moodle, ILIAS; **OpenOlat** in German-speaking HE | Mixed | **Moodle, Chamilo** |
| Open source supply | Modest (OATutor); `canvas-mcp` 317★ serves the Canvas-dominant market | 🟢 **Upgraded at p653: the only region that owns a permissive institutional *platform* — `OpenOlat`, Apache-2.0, 449★ / 32,210 commits, `langchain4j` in-tree.** Agent layer still thin (`feedbacksystem` 24★; Moodle bridges cap at 27★) | **Dominant — DeepTutor, OpenMAIC, AI-Shifu, and now the model layer (OmniEdu)** | Real but **licence-nil** (`Edu-mentor-AI`); no LATAM education agent repo found in any pass |
| Primary sell | Compliance + Canvas agents | Conformity by design; Gulf capability builds; **offline-first in Africa** | **Conformity by design (Korea)** + co-development + labelling | **Governance first, small and fixed-fee** |

🔵 **The single most useful shift in this table at p651:** the EMEA conformity-by-design offer is no longer region-specific. South Korea's AI Basic Act names student assessment in the high-impact category and its enforcement edge (≈ January 2027) falls **before** the EU's (2 December 2027). The same engagement shape now has two jurisdictions and two dates, with Korea the earlier one.

🔴 **And the shift at p652 inverts the assumption underneath this whole page: the EU is now the *last* of three binding education-AI deadlines, not the forcing function.** Ordered:

| Date | Jurisdiction | What falls due |
|---|---|---|
| **≈ Jan 2027** | South Korea | Administrative fines become available after MSIT's signalled grace period; student assessment in early-childhood/elementary/secondary education is high-impact by statute |
| **1 Sep 2027** | Vietnam | Compliance deadline for education systems already operating before 15 Aug 2026 (health and finance share the date; other sectors 1 Mar 2027) |
| **2 Dec 2027** | EU | Stand-alone Annex III high-risk obligations, after the Digital Omnibus deferral from 2 Aug 2026 |

Three consequences, and they are not cosmetic. **(1)** A client selling education AI into all three markets needs one conformity programme with three jurisdictional profiles, sequenced to the Korean date — not an EU programme with APAC bolted on. **(2)** Any proposal that frames December 2027 as "the deadline" is eleven months late for a client with Korean exposure. **(3)** The EU deferral bought European clients runway and bought APAC clients nothing, so the regions that looked least regulated on this page a year ago are now the ones with the nearest dates. **The habit of treating EMEA as the compliance region and APAC as the supply region is now wrong in both directions** — APAC supplies the open source *and* has the earliest deadlines.

## 🔴 Declared gaps — searched and not found

### 🆕 Added at p719 — four gaps, each from a leg that RAN, plus one date this base must not state with confidence

🔴 **`719-G1` — no 2026 education-specific ADOPTION statistics for APAC.** The APAC leg returned a dense, well-sourced regulatory picture (Korea's AI Basic Act in force, Vietnam's high-risk education designation, China's labelling regime, Australia's pending guardrails) and **not one teacher- or student-level usage rate for 2026.** Every APAC number available to this pass was regulatory or vendor-commercial. North America has Gallup's `60%` of teachers; LATAM has UNESCO IESALC's `87%` across `19` countries; EMEA has the `~10%`-with-guidelines reading. **APAC — the region with the largest cohort on this base's agent shelf — has no equivalent.** Not filled with a global figure standing in for a regional one.

🔴 **`719-G2` — NOTHING on this base addresses TEACHER WORKLOAD AND BURNOUT, which is the benefit that actually gets funded.** Faculty Focus names rising teacher workload and burnout as the pressure AI adoption is expected to relieve, **mainly by cutting administrative demands** rather than by improving instruction — and administrative-burden reduction is the business case a school board approves when an instructional-gain case stalls. Measured across the three shelves and the trends file: `teacher workload` **`0` occurrences**, `burnout` **`0` occurrences**. Not thin — absent. 🔴 **Paired absence in the same probe: `contract cheating` `0` and `authorship verification` `0`, while `proctor` returns `12` hits in [`trends.md`](trends.md) alone.** So this base covers integrity *surveillance* well and integrity *provenance* not at all, which is the half institutions are actually moving toward.

🔵 **`719-G2b` — an instrument correction on this very entry, recorded because the near-miss is the lesson.** p719's first draft of `G2` asserted that these shelves hold *nothing whose subject is a credential*, citing 1EdTech's skills-pathways-and-digital-credentials trend. **That claim was FALSE and a grep caught it before it shipped:** the base holds [`1EdTech/OpenCASE`](https://github.com/1EdTech/OpenCASE), [`1EdTech/cert-schema`](https://github.com/1EdTech/cert-schema) and [`1EdTech/digital-credentials-public-validator`](https://github.com/1EdTech/digital-credentials-public-validator), plus `Open Badges`, `xAPI` and `ESCO` references across all three shelves, and `p718` already wired them into pattern `P130`. 🔴 **The transferable rule: a declared gap is a CLAIM ABOUT THIS BASE'S OWN CONTENTS and must be grepped against the shelves before it is written, exactly as a filed row is verified against the bytes.** An unchecked gap is worse than an unchecked row — it tells the next pass to spend a leg rediscovering what is already held, and `p703` recorded that failure once already in the opposite direction.

🔴 **`719-G3` — no binding national AI-in-education law surfaced anywhere in LATAM.** The leg ran and returned policy direction (UNESCO's recommendation set, the April 2026 Santiago Observatory) rather than enforceable duties. **Brazil's general AI bill and Chile's AI framework both exist but were not resolved to education-specific obligations inside this pass's budget**, and are named as the next LATAM regulatory question rather than guessed at.

🔴 **`719-G4` — two platform subjects could not be resolved to a canonical repository.** `Fedena` and `Forma LMS` appeared in the verticals leg via third-party listicles only; neither resolved to a verifiable canonical repository with a readable grant. Named so the next pass resolves them deliberately rather than rediscovering them in a listicle.

🔴 **`719-D` — AND THE ONE THIS BASE MUST NOT STATE WITH CONFIDENCE: the EU AI Act Annex III date for education.** Sources split between **2 August 2026** and **2 December 2027** (a 2026 "Digital Omnibus" postponement, independently described as agreed politically in May 2026). The later date is carried by the more current sources, but **p719 verified neither against the Official Journal and will not pretend to have.** Full treatment under `## Opportunities by region → EMEA`. 🔴 **Both failure modes are expensive and neither is recoverable by a later correction: advising to a date that moved costs a client a rushed programme; advising to a postponement that did not survive costs them non-compliance.** Until the primary text is read, every EU engagement statement must carry both dates.

Six permissive integrity addresses in total, all re-verified first-hand at p661. 🔴 **The corrected reading is not "the gap is closed":** the strongest is `633★` and dormant since `2024-12-10`, the live ones run `72★ / 48★ / 37★ / 3★`, and proctoring is **EU AI Act Annex III high-risk** with emotion recognition in schools prohibited since February 2025. **Prior art to read, not a base to ship.** See `intel/trends.md` **T40**, where this becomes the fifth consecutive supply claim to turn out to be an adoption gap.

- 🔵 **p658 — the mandated web legs are nil for the SIXTEENTH consecutive pass, and this time one leg was actively WRONG.** All eight mandated searches were run (four global, one per region) and scored against HEAD with a denominator: **34 facts tested, 34 already held, 0 adoptable** — Korea's AI Basic Act and its MSIT grace period, Vietnam's six-sector high-risk decision, Singapore's AI Risk Management Guidelines, Regulation (EU) 2026/1744 with its 2 December 2027 and 2 August 2028 dates, the UK DfE January 2026 product-safety standards, Ofqual's March 2026 integrity resources, KHDA Dubai, Europe at $2.64B/31.9% CAGR, the OECD *Digital Education Outlook 2026*, the US 134-bills-across-31-states count, Maryland SB 720, New York A 9190, Ohio's July 2026 deadline, California AB 1159 and SB 243, the K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act, the student-authored STUDENTS FIRST Act, Gallup's 60% teacher figure, the $169M federal higher-education investment, AASA's 50-state framework, Ceibal's 75%, Cetic.br's 65% and 53%/22%, UNESCO IESALC's 26% of 200 institutions across 19 countries, the Universidad de los Andes count of 716 instruments across 23 countries, the UNESCO LAC Observatory of 14 April 2026, and the Tecnológico de Monterrey and CENIA partnerships. A nonsense control was scored in the same run and correctly returned NOVEL, so the instrument was not simply answering HELD to everything — which is p647-L's defect class, deliberately re-tested.
- 🔴 **The EMEA/APAC leg returned a claim this base has already tested and REJECTED — the third instance of the adversarial pattern, and the first repeat offender.** The leg reproduced, from a law-firm summary, the assertion that **TEQSA requires all Australian higher education providers to submit institutional action plans addressing generative-AI risks.** p652 tested that claim and found no such submission mandate (guidance plus Threshold Standard 5.2, not an instrument), and the APAC block above already records it as rejected. **Adopting this pass's leg output verbatim would have re-introduced a known-false regulatory claim into the shelf.** Taken with p651's North Carolina date rejection and p652's original TEQSA rejection, the regional legs have now produced three negative results and zero additive ones in a row: **their value is adversarial, and the correct way to run them is append-and-compare, never overwrite.**
- 🔴 **Caliper: the absence is now a MEASUREMENT rather than a nil result.** This page has recorded for several passes that no Caliper (1EdTech analytics) implementation exists "at any address this base can find". `topic:caliper-analytics` was swept at p658 — the first pass to query that head — and returns **exactly two addresses**, `lsloan/Repilac` (1★, last pushed **2016-08-12**) and `lsloan/udp-notebooks` (0★, last pushed **2020-11-19**), **both grant-nil** and both dormant for six to ten years. The conclusion is unchanged and better supported: there is no second telemetry format to support, xAPI has won outright, and the gap is now stated with its denominator instead of as silence.
- 🔴 **No LATAM-origin address in the learn-to-code layer** — 21 verified addresses, origins North America / EMEA / APAC only. Fourth consecutive pass to measure a thin LATAM supply side. See the LATAM block above, where it is written down as an absence rather than padded.
- 🔧 **[SUPERSEDED AT p661 — see the p661 correction at the top of this section. The channel diagnosis below is still correct and still useful; the "without a permissive answer" headline is withdrawn.]** 🔴 **Accessibility remediation remains without a permissive answer, and p658 narrows WHY the searches keep failing.** `topic:wcag` and `topic:captioning` were swept this pass and are **homonym-polluted to the point of uselessness** for this question: `wcag` returns colour-contrast and design-token libraries (`tomloprod/colority`, `metasidd/ColorTokensKit-Swift`, `soffes/Color`) and a CAPTCHA widget (`altcha-org/altcha`, 2,877★) as its highest-starred results; `captioning` returns image-captioning ML models, not caption authoring for media. The genuinely on-domain permissive results are component libraries for building accessible UIs (`incluud/accessible-astro-*`, MIT; `ng-primitives`, Apache-2.0; `cvs-health/ios-swiftui-accessibility-techniques`, Apache-2.0) and a UK government guide (`alphagov/guide-to-wcag`, MIT) — **none of which remediates existing course content**, which is the actual demand. `ucfopen/UDOIT` (GPL-3.0) is still the only production tool. The gap stands; what is new is that **the keyword channel cannot reach it**, so a future pass should query by capability, not by tag.

- 🟢 **Closed at p656 on the supply side, and replaced by a narrower gap: outcome measurement.** This page and `intel/trends.md` have recorded for many passes that the sector cannot show its results. The permissive substrate exists — xAPI is an Apache-2.0 ratified specification and [`yetanalytics/lrsql`](https://github.com/yetanalytics/lrsql) is an Apache-2.0, actively maintained Learning Record Store (144 ★, pushed 2026-11-03). 🔴 **The replacement gap is that there is exactly one of it**: `LearningLocker/learninglocker` is GPL-3.0 and dormant since 2024-05-30, `adlnet/ADL_LRS` is Apache-2.0 but ~12 months quiet. Any measurement proposal on a permissive stack carries a single-vendor dependency and should name it.
- 🔧 **[WITHDRAWN AT p661 — three of the four capabilities this mandate creates have permissive answers; only in-place WCAG repair inside an LMS does not. See the p661 correction at the top of this section.]** 🔴 **No permissive answer to accessibility remediation — searched this pass and genuinely absent.** `topic:accessibility topic:education` (442 repositories) yields one production tool, `ucfopen/UDOIT`, and it is **GPL-3.0**. This is the only named demand driver on this page with no permissive supply. See `intel/trends.md` **T29**.
- 🔴 **No `Caliper` (1EdTech analytics) implementation exists in open source at any address this base can find** — not on a shelf, not in the append-only logs, not in the p656 topic sweeps. xAPI has won the learning-analytics interoperability layer outright. Stated as a measurement, because an absent competitor standard is a planning fact: there is no second telemetry format to support.
- 🔴 **Two specification repositories ship without grant files.** `1EdTech/openbadges-specification` (205 ★) and `Lumieducation/Lumi` (196 ★) both 404 on six root grant filenames on their real default branches. For Open Badges the intent is presumably permissive; the grant is absent, and absent is what a client's counsel will find.
- 🔴 **No LATAM-origin repository surfaced in any of the four topic sweeps run at p656** (`xapi`, `lti`, `scorm`, `accessibility+education`). The two permissive rows shelved this pass with identifiable provenance are both EMEA. Recorded as a measured absence.
- ⚠️ **The South Korean teacher-AI-training figure (~US$740M, 2024–2026) added at p656 is secondary** — market-research and vendor compilations, not a Korean government budget document. Not reconciled to a primary source; treat as an indication that the mandate is funded rather than as a quotable number.
- 🔴 **`huggingface.co` has not resolved for five consecutive passes** (`CONNECT tunnel failed, 403`), so OmniEdu's weight licence remains unread. A standing limitation of this session's egress policy rather than an incident. 🔵 **One channel finding worth carrying forward:** `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` returns 403 for every repository not attached to this session, while `gh api search/repositories -f q='repo:{owner}/{repo}'` returns full metadata and accepts many `repo:` qualifiers per call. Never conclude from a `repos/` 403 that an address is dead.

- **No education-specific AI regulation found for Brazil, Mexico or Chile.** All three have horizontal AI bills pending; none has published education-sector rules. Searched; genuinely absent, not merely unlocated. 🟢 **p654 strengthens this from an absence to a measurement for Brazil:** the ANPD's pilot AI regulatory sandbox — the country's one operating AI-governance instrument — admitted up to three participants under Notice 2/2025 and **none of them is an education project** (mobility routing, a medical LLM chatbot, and an AI-agent security layer; the third slot is disputed across sources). The gap is not that Brazilian education AI rules are unlocated. It is that the regulator's only live sandbox has no education participant to write them from.
- 🟢 **Partly closed: APAC now has one education-specific binding rule.** South Korea's AI Basic Act names student assessment in early-childhood, elementary and secondary education inside its high-impact definition. The gap as previously stated ("no education-specific AI rules surfaced for China, India or Japan") still holds for those three countries, and the named-player hole in APAC on the demand side is unchanged: the regional player lists available are vendor market-research rankings (Google, Microsoft, IBM, Pearson, Byju's, Squirrel AI), not procurement-grade intelligence.
- 🟢 **Closed at p652: country-level African AI policy.** The gap as previously stated — "country-level education AI regulation for any Sub-Saharan African state" — is resolved into an accurate statement rather than left as silence: **national AI strategies with explicit education commitments exist (Kenya 2025–2030, Nigeria's digital economy strategy), and education-sector rules do not.** South Africa's draft National AI Policy was published 10 April 2026 and withdrawn seventeen days later. The African Union's own timeline puts Phase 1 (2025–2026) at establishing governance structures and national strategies, so the continent is at the strategy stage **by design, on schedule** — this is not a lag to be explained, it is where the roadmap says it should be. Kenya's unwritten AI-literacy framework is a named, unfilled deliverable inside a published strategy. African *open-source supply* remains genuinely absent (p651): builders ship proprietary apps on open weights.
- 🟢 **Closed at p652: the Canadian half of the North America leg**, after three consecutive passes of this page admitting that "North America" meant the United States. Canada now has its own block above: the 4 June 2026 national AI strategy, the National AI Literacy Initiative (1M students, 3,000+ educators), the absence of any AI Act after AIDA died on prorogation in January 2025, and K-12 policy ranging from Quebec's suspension directive to Nova Scotia's nothing. **The leg is hereby re-scoped to the United States and Canada explicitly**, and Mexico is covered under LATAM, which resolves the filing inconsistency this page flagged. 🔴 **Still open: Mexican *education-specific* material.** Mexico appears only through capacity-building and horizontal-bill sources (INFOTEC/TecNM training centre, the February 2026 risk-classification bill); no Mexican education AI rule or adoption survey has surfaced in any pass.
- 🔴 **One claim was tested and rejected rather than published** — the North Carolina SL 2026-41 model-policy dates. Recorded in `intel/measured-unusable.md`. This is the first pass in a long run where a mandated web leg produced a *negative* result worth keeping: the leg's value was in stopping a wrong date from entering the KB, not in adding a right one.
- **Market-size figures were not reconciled to primary research reports.** All are secondary citations of vendor and market-research marketing. p652 adds two more secondary figures without reconciling them either, and they disagree with each other and with this page's existing range: the AI-education market at **~$11.4B in 2026 → $57.25B by 2033** (Coherent Market Insights), against other 2026 compilations citing ~$12.3B for 2026 and $136.79B by 2035. **Europe is quoted at $2.64B (2026) → $8.0B (2030) at a 31.9% CAGR** — the 2030 endpoint and CAGR are new; the 2026 figure reproduces what this page already held. Treat all of these as definitional artefacts, not measurements, and never put two of them in the same sentence.
- 🔴 **Five hosts did not resolve under this session's network policy at p652**, and the claims that depend on them are marked secondary rather than presented as read: `www.unesco.org` and `www.iesalc.unesco.org` (the LATAM IESALC figures — independently re-derived from search, and they reproduce), `www.oecd.org` (the Digital Education Outlook 2026 recommendations, quoted below from search excerpts of the PDF rather than from the PDF), `www.dlapiper.com` (the Canadian strategy analysis), and `huggingface.co` (OmniEdu's weight licence — which is why `agents/top.md` gates that row). This is the second consecutive pass where a network restriction, not a research failure, set the ceiling on first-hand verification for specific claims. It is cheap to fix and worth fixing: allowing `oecd.org`, `unesco.org` and `huggingface.co` would convert several of this KB's load-bearing secondary citations into primary ones.
- 🟢 **A negative result worth keeping, for the second consecutive pass.** p651 rejected the North Carolina SL 2026-41 model-policy dates. p652 rejected the claim that **TEQSA requires Australian higher education providers to submit institutional gen-AI action plans** — it does not; TEQSA publishes guidance and leans on Threshold Standard 5.2. Two passes, two mandated web legs whose value was stopping a wrong claim rather than adding a right one. That is now a pattern rather than an anomaly, and it argues for keeping the regional legs even in passes where they look unproductive: **the regional leg's job is partly adversarial.**

🟢 **Strengthened at p662 — the licence-truth deliverable now has a second, sharper specimen.** A currently published open-source-LMS comparison table places **`LearnHouse` in its "MIT/Apache … maximum flexibility" column; the repository serves `AGPL-3.0`** (`34,523 B`, `sha256₁₂ 8486a10c4393`, identical on `dev` and `main`, `gh api` agrees). 🔵 This is a better sales specimen than the `TurboWarp/extensions` case because the error runs in the **expensive** direction: a studio that trusted the table and shipped a hosted, client-branded fork has acquired a source-disclosure obligation it never priced. The deliverable is unchanged — resolve the real default ref, byte-read the grant, state the digest — and the pitch is now *"here is a live table that would have cost you the engagement."* Full refutation on `intel/measured-unusable.md`.

### 🆕 Added at p716 — three gaps, each the result of a leg that RAN and returned nothing

🔴 **`G-716-1` — No region-wide APAC AI-in-schools regulatory framework, and no verified list of leading APAC AI-education companies.** The mandated APAC regional leg ran in full and returned neither. The only binding instruments it surfaced were the **UK's** generative-AI school safety standards (January 2026) and the **UAE's** `25` generative-AI prohibitions (February 2026) — **both EMEA**, and both refiled to the EMEA section. 🔵 **The gap is in the CHANNEL, not in the territory:** `p715` established that the first binding instrument in the region naming education as high-risk is **Vietnam's**, which this leg did not surface at all. **An English-language keyword leg cannot see national-ministry instruments published in national languages.** Closing this requires probing ministries directly — China, Singapore, Australia, South Korea, Japan, Vietnam, India — and the keyword leg should not be re-run expecting a different answer.

🔴 **`G-716-2` — The Spanish/Portuguese keyword channel for LATAM repository supply is EXHAUSTED.** `tutor inteligencia artificial educacion license:mit` returned **`0`** results; `educacao inteligencia artificial aluno license:mit` returned **`1`**, last pushed `2025-05-17` and stale by `18` months. 🟢 **Recorded as an exhausted channel rather than as an absence of supply**, because the same pass placed `4` LATAM subjects through a different shape — searching the **institution's own system name** (`e-Disciplinas`, `JupiterWeb`, `SUAP`, `AVA`, `e-FICH`). See `716-D` and `P123`.

🔴 **`G-716-3` — No binding regional AI-in-education regulation in LATAM, and little K-12 policy evidence anywhere in the region's literature.** Mexico shows documented calls for regulatory alignment, institutional reform and curriculum redesign, but **calls are not instruments.** The region's available evidence is heavily weighted toward **higher education**: the two largest instruments this base holds (the DEC/Tec de Monterrey faculty-and-student survey, UNESCO IESALC's `200`-institution study across `19` countries) are both tertiary. **K-12 policy in LATAM is therefore an unmeasured area, not a quiet one**, and any K-12 claim this base makes about the region should be treated as unsupported until a primary source is found.
